  - For 𝒜 = H₁B̃, (3.133) with PBG's common rate δ₀ and constant B₀ (p.423 l.39–40) and the factor a_{j′}^{−1} of the data gives, at x ∈ Δ(y), y ∈ Λ_j, a_j|𝒜(x)| + a_j²|∇𝒜(x)| ≤ 2B₀Σ_{y′}(a_j/a_{j′})e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)}|B̃(y′)|, the sum over the points y′ of the data, whose measure a_{j′}^d cancels the a_{j′}^{−d} of (3.133). Since a_j/a_{j′} ≤ L^{2(j−j′)_+} and |B̃| ≤ (1 + 2dL)b_k, the row sums c_K of (b) bound this by σ_k := 2B₀c_K(1 + 2dL)b_k. With |D𝒜| ≤ 2|∇𝒜| and d(d − 1)/2 plaquettes at a point, Σ_{x∈Δ(y)}η⁴|D𝒜(x)|² and sup_{Δ(y)}(a_j|𝒜|)² are both ≤ N_k := 2d²σ_k² per point y.
  - The form ⟨H_{1,k}B′, Δ₁(ζ₀)H_{1,k}B′⟩ of (1.2) is the second-order part in B′ of g_k^{−2}A(ζ₀, U_{k,Z}(V′V_Λ)), since V(ζ₀, ·) is at least of third order (p.357 l.9). In each run write U_{k,Z}(V′V_Λ) = e^{iη(g_k𝒜₁ + g_k²𝒜₂ + O(g_k³))}U_0, with 𝒜₁ = H_{1,k}B′ and 𝒜₂ the second-order data derivative. For a weight w let Δ^{act}(w) be the plain second variation of A(w, ·) at U_0 (PBG (3.10) with the weight w(p) on each plaquette) and J(w) its current, ⟨X, J(w)⟩ := dA(w, U_0)[X]. Then the form is ⟨𝒜₁, Δ^{act}(ζ₀)𝒜₁⟩ + 2⟨𝒜₂, J(ζ₀)⟩. The expansion is taken in VAR's gauge (153), in which the double-bar constraint (156) holds with the twisted data, so 𝒜₁ = H₁B̃′ by *The lifts and the chain rule.*; the second-order coefficient does not depend on the gauge, since each plaquette term of A(ζ₀, ·) is gauge invariant. By (1.4) (p.357 l.19–26) the unweighted current J_0 = J(1) is 𝐐*λ_0, with 𝐐 the plain multiscale averaging and λ_0 a field on the data of Z∖Λ, and by *The interface twist.* J_0 = Q*ν_0 with Q := Q_db and ν_0 = (1 − Ξ)*λ_0. Let C^{(2)} be the second-order term of the multiscale double bar (PBG p.421, after (3.127)) and B̃₂ the second-order part in B′ of the twisted data. Then Q𝒜₂ + C^{(2)}[𝒜₁, 𝒜₁] = B̃₂, so ⟨𝒜₂, J_0⟩ = ⟨B̃₂ − C^{(2)}[𝒜₁, 𝒜₁], ν_0⟩. Since A(ζ₀, ·) + A(1 − ζ₀, ·) is the unweighted action, J(ζ₀) = J_0 − J(1 − ζ₀), and

    ⟨H_{1,k}B′, Δ₁(ζ₀)H_{1,k}B′⟩ = f(𝒜₁) + 2⟨B̃₂, ν_0⟩ − 2⟨𝒜₂, J(1 − ζ₀)⟩, f(𝒜) := ⟨𝒜, Δ^{act}(ζ₀)𝒜⟩ − 2⟨C^{(2)}[𝒜, 𝒜], ν_0⟩.

    The bulk form f consists of the action part of the Cauchy note's §10.4, with the weight ζ₀, and its constraint part. The identity is the Taylor expansion itself, so no π-part and no gauge-fixing term enter it. The third term is the part carried by the weight 1 − ζ₀, below. The second, the twist term, vanishes unless Λ meets an interface. Off Λ the plain data is fixed, so B̃₂ vanishes off Λ and off the bonds of S_j whose blocks B(x) meet Λ. On the data of Λ, λ_0 = 0, so ν_0 = −Ξ*λ_0 there, which vanishes off the bonds read by Ξ. So 2⟨B̃₂, ν_0⟩ = 0 when no block B(x) of an outer endpoint of S_j meets Λ. Otherwise its run difference is open: it needs the second-order part of (154) in B′, the Baker–Campbell–Hausdorff terms of the contour holonomies and of the twist, and its run difference, per interface point at the size (ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}N_k; the multiplier bounds of *The multipliers.* would then bound the pairing, but neither LFR II, which works with the plain constraint, nor the Cauchy note, which is single-scale, states these terms. At the pair (Ũ, J′) of the alignment, Ũ = MU^B_0 and J′ = Q_db(Ũ)*ν^B (*The intermediate current and the estimate.*), so run A's bulk form f^A has ⟨C_A^{(2)}[X, X], ν^B⟩ = ⟨H^AC_A^{(2)}[X, X], J′⟩ in its constraint term, for every X, since Q_db(Ũ)H^A = 1. By (B4) of §10.1 on each layer, and on S_j since the twist reads the double bars of the finer layer, the multiscale double bar composes as Q^B_db(W) = Q^A_db(M̄(W)), with M̄ the one-step double bar. So the second-order chain rule C_B^{(2)}[𝒜, 𝒜] = C_A^{(2)}[Q₁𝒜, Q₁𝒜] + Q^AC₁^{(2)}[𝒜, 𝒜] holds for the multiscale double bar, with Q₁ := Q̄₁(U^B) and C₁^{(2)} := C̄₁^{(2)}, the quadratic part of CMP 98 (122), throughout (d). The weighted actions are the same under M̄ and M, plaquette by plaquette, since the two differ by the gauge transformation v₁ of (B1). With Q̄₁ = Q₁^{pl} + D̄Θ₁, the term y_p of (10.6a) gains [F̄_p, Θ₁𝒜], with |Θ₁𝒜| ≤ c_vε|𝒜| by CMP 98 (162)–(163), which is of the relative size (ε/a_j)ε₁ of y_p itself and enters C_d. Then the action and constraint parts of §10.4 give, for β_f(𝒜, 𝒜) := f^A(Q₁𝒜) − f^B(𝒜),

    β_f(𝒜, 𝒜) = d²b_{ζ₀}(U^B_0)[𝒜, 𝒜] − 2⟨J^A(ζ₀) − J′, C₁^{(2)}[𝒜, 𝒜]⟩,

    with J^A(ζ₀) the current of A_ε(ζ₀, ·) at Ũ and b_w(W) := A_ε(w, MW) − A_{ε/L}(w, W) the weighted blocking artifact. By the column sums, b_w is the expansion of (10.6a) with the weight w(p) on each coarse term, plus the weight-gradient sum −½Σ_pε⁴Σ_{p′}w_{p,p′}(w(p′) − w(p))|F_{p′}|² and the quartic terms with the same weights. Besides the terms of (10.6a) and the new term above, the second variation of the weight-gradient sum and the current term give two pieces.
    - The curvature piece of the weight-gradient sum, −Σ_pε⁴Σ_{p′}w_{p,p′}(w(p′) − w(p))·2⟨F_{p′}, [𝒜 ∧ 𝒜]_{p′}⟩ up to higher orders, is ≤ 2C_ζ(ε/a_n)·C₀ε₁a_j^{−2}·2|𝒜|² per fine plaquette by (9.2) at the scale j, so ≤ 2d²C₀C_ζ(ε/a_n)ε₁N_k on the fine cells of Δ(y).
    - The current term. J^A(ζ₀)(b) = ε^{−1}Σ_{p∋b}±ζ₀(p)F_p, transported, and the 2(d − 1) plaquettes at b pair into differences at the spacing ε. So |J^A(ζ₀)| ≤ 2(d − 1)(|∇F| + |∇ζ₀||F|) ≤ c_wε₁a_j^{−3}, c_w := 4(d − 1)(1 + C)C₀, by |F| ≤ 2C₀ε₁/a_j² and |∇F| ≤ 2C₀ε₁/a_j³ for Ũ (§9.3) and |∇ζ₀| ≤ C/a_k ≤ C/a_j. The alignment gives a_j³|J′| ≤ B̄_Jε₁. With |C₁^{(2)}[𝒜, 𝒜]| ≤ C₂ε|𝒜|² (VAR (44)), the current term is ≤ 2d(c_w + B̄_J)C₂ε₁(ε/a_j)N_k on the fine cells of Δ(y).
  - With (3.133) at the layer of p and the exponent γ_d, the part of |β_w(𝒜^B, 𝒜^B)| on the fine cells of Δ(y), y ∈ Λ_j, and the same part of |β_f(𝒜^B, 𝒜^B)|, are ≤ C_d[(ε/a_j)^{γ_d} + C_ζ(ε/a_n) + ε₁(ε/a_j)]N_k. Here ε₁ ≤ 1 by (T-Λ), and C_d := C_β(γ_d) of (10.6a) plus 2d²C₀ plus 2d(c_w + B̄_J)C₂ plus the constants of the Cauchy note's (10.6c) at γ_d, so that C_d also covers the two pieces above and bounds the gauge-fixing sources below.
  - The part carried by the weight 1 − ζ₀, −2⟨𝒜₂, J(1 − ζ₀)⟩, is not of the form of §10.4: J(1 − ζ₀) need not annihilate ker Q, so the constraint does not remove 𝒜₂. The main proof does not compare it separately: the form of (1.2) enters only through the denominator d of (1.1), which *The complementary-weight parts.* below compares as a whole (derived).
- *The terms in E.* Put 𝒜^X := H^X₁B̃^X, with each run's twisted data B̃^X := (1 + Ξ_X)B at the common B, E := Q₁𝒜^B − 𝒜^A with Q₁ = Q̄₁(U^B), and F_w^X(𝒜) := Σ_pη_X⁴w(p)|(D𝒜)(p)|². Then
  F_w^A(𝒜^A) − F_w^B(𝒜^B) = β_w(𝒜^B, 𝒜^B) − 2F_w^A(E, Q₁𝒜^B) + F_w^A(E), and likewise f^A(𝒜^A) − f^B(𝒜^B) = β_f(𝒜^B, 𝒜^B) − 2f^A(E, Q₁𝒜^B) + f^A(E).
  - Unlike in (10.5), the terms in E are of first order, since H₁ minimises the unweighted form.
  - Q^AE = B̃^B − B̃^A = (Ξ_B − Ξ_A)B, so E = E₀ + E_Ξ with E₀ := (I − H^A₁Q^A)E ∈ ker Q^A and E_Ξ := H^A₁(Ξ_B − Ξ_A)B. By the entry bounds of *The interface twist.*, |((Ξ_B − Ξ_A)B)(b)| ≤ 12d²L²d̄ max|B| on S_j, so (3.133) with the count of (b) gives a_{j(x)}|E_Ξ(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇E_Ξ(x)| ≤ 12d²L²d̄σ_k ≤ 12d²L²C_U(M)(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁σ_k. By §10.4 and *The lifts and the chain rule.*, ⟨ψ, G_A^{−1}E⟩ = β(ℒψ, 𝒜^B) for ψ ∈ ker Q^A, with β the unweighted defect, and ⟨ψ, G₁^{−1}H^A₁Q^AE⟩ = 0. So E₀ = G₁^Qℒ*(D*s₁ + s₀), with G₁^Q := (I − H₁Q)G₁ at run A's pair, where β(ℱ, 𝒜^B) = ⟨Dℱ, s₁⟩ + ⟨ℱ, s₀⟩; PBG's 𝔊 of (3.153) is not used, as in the Cauchy note's §10.4. The variance term gives s₁(p′) := Σ_pL⁴w_{p,p′}(X_{p′} − X̄_p), a probability average with |s₁(p′)| ≤ the oscillation of X over the 2L × 2L fine squares meeting p′, a set within 4ε of p′. The r-terms give pieces of s₁ of the size of y_p and of that oscillation. The curvature couplings and the current term give s₀. The gauge-fixing part gives, by the Cauchy note's (10.6c), a piece of s₀ and the sources D s₂ and ∇*s₁^{gf}, with |s₂| and |s₁^{gf}| of the relative size of s₁; the bounds of G₁^Q of the Cauchy note's §10.4, those of (3.42) for G₁ entry by entry with B₀ + 2c_δ′B₀² in place of B₀, here at the multiscale (3.132)–(3.133) of *The pair class.* with the transfer of the bounds above, treat them as they treat D*s₁. The π-part of β is open for multiscale data. By (B5) and the cancellation of the Cauchy note's §10.4, *The π-Part of β.*, it is a pairing −⟨q_X(μ_A, A) + q_X(μ_A, P_XA), D̄*ν_π⟩ with ν_π := 𝒬′*λ^B of that paragraph, which vanishes in the single-scale case, where D̄*ν_π = 0. For multiscale data D̄*ν_π ≠ 0 at the sites of ∂Ω_{j+1} where two layers meet, and the divergences bullet of *The adjoint averaging across layers.* gives only |D̄*ν_π| ≤ 2dL^{−d}B̄_λε₁a_j^{−4} there, of relative size ε₁. What is missing is the bound |⟨q_X(μ_A, A) + q_X(μ_A, P_XA), D̄*ν_π⟩| ≤ C(ε/a_n)^{γ_d}N_k per interface point. Neither source supplies it: PBG computes (3.114)–(3.115) at U₁ = 1 (p.418), and the Cauchy note's cancellation is single-scale. The gauge-fixing sources use the multiscale form of (10.6c) of the bullet above, with a_{j(x)} for ℓ at the point x and τ̂_n for τ̂.
  - This is the residual of (b) with s₁ and s₀ in place of r₁ and r₀. A source at a point of the layer j′ has relative size ≤ C_d(ε/a_{j′})^{γ_d}, in place of C_r(ε/a_{j′})ε₁, and the gradient bound (3.44) costs the Hölder factor 2(a_{j′}/ε)^{ε₀}. So, for the sources other than the π-part, a_{j(x)}|E₀(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇E₀(x)| ≤ x_E(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/2}σ_k, with σ_k the layer-weighted supremum of 𝒜^B above (the sources at y′ are sized by 𝒜^B near y′), x_E := C_G^Q(ε₀)c_KC_d, and γ_d − ε₀ = (1 − ε₀)/2. Here C_G^Q(ε₀) is the Cauchy note's C_G(ε₀) = 4c_𝒦C_r(B₀ + B₀′(ε₀)) with B₀ + 2c_δ′B₀² in place of B₀; its factor C_r is not needed here, and x_E is taken with max(C_r, 1) in place of C_r, so that it is an upper bound in either case. With E_Ξ, ε₁ ≤ 1, and (ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/2} ≤ (ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}, a_{j(x)}|E(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇E(x)| ≤ x̂_E(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}σ_k, x̂_E := x_E + 12d²L²C_U(M).
  - By Cauchy–Schwarz on each Δ(y), |F_w^A(E, Q₁𝒜^B)| ≤ x̂_E(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}N_k per point, and F_w^A(E) ≤ x̂_E²(ε/a_n)^{2(1−ε₀)/3}N_k.
  - For f^A two further terms are of relative size ε₁. The curvature terms of PBG (3.10), with |F| ≤ 2C₀ε₁/a_j² at Ũ, add at most 2(1 + d²)C₀ε₁ times these bounds. The constraint term is ⟨C_A^{(2)}[X, Y], ν^B⟩ = ⟨H^AC_A^{(2)}[X, Y], J′⟩, by J′ = Q_db(Ũ)*ν^B, with |C_A^{(2)}[X, Y]| ≤ C₂a_{j′}|X||Y| at a point of the layer j′ (VAR (44) at the block scale), H^A bounded by (3.133) as for 𝒜 above, and a_j³|J′| ≤ B̄_Jε₁. With the row sums c_K it adds at most 4dB₀c_KC₂B̄_Jε₁ times these bounds. So the bounds hold for f^A with the factor 1 + c_bfε₁, c_bf := 2(1 + d²)C₀ + 4dB₀c_KC₂B̄_J; the name c_bf keeps it apart from the Cauchy note's c_f.
- *The complementary-weight parts.* The parts −2⟨𝒜₂, J(1 − ζ₀)⟩ of the form of (1.2) and −2⟨𝒜₂, J(1 − ζ)⟩ of the small form of (1.20) need no comparison of their own. The form of (1.2) enters the main proof only through the denominator d of (1.1), and the small form of (1.20) only through the sum 𝐂_k of LFR II p.369 l.2–7. Both are compared below through the weighted actions of (b) and (c), with no use of 𝒜₂, E₂, or the remainders V(ζ₀, ·) and V(ζ, ·), so bounds of 𝒜₂ and E₂ are not needed. The comparison of d and of 𝐂_k as a whole is derived. LFR II then localises 𝐂_k and re-sums its pieces into V(X^∼) and V(Y) (p.373 l.10–17, (1.59)–(1.64), (1.68)), and the comparison of these pieces, *The Localised Pieces of 𝐂_k* below, is open. The comparisons are at real configurations and at equal shared variables and couplings, as in (b)–(d), except in the item on the couplings.
  - *The denominator.* By (1.1) (p.356 l.19–20), d = ∫δ_{G₀}(V′)χ exp[−g_k^{−2}A(ζ₀, U_{k,Z}(V′V_Λ))] dV′|_Λ, where Bałaban's V_Λ carries on Λ the data of U_0 (LFR I (1.79), p.195). So V′ is the deviation from this data, V′ = 1 gives U_0, and the cutoff χ̄, the soft form of χ(|(1/i)log V′(b)| < M₀ε_k) (LFR I (1.101), p.201; §3(d)), is centred there. In the notation of (c), run X's integrand at V′ is exp[−g_k^{−2}A_X(ζ₀, U^X_{k,Z}(V′V′^X₀V_Λ))], with V_Λ the shared data off Λ. At equal V′ the two runs' data on Λ^{(k)} differs by e^{iX}, since V′V′^B₀ = V′e^{iX}V′^A₀ and the norm is bi-invariant. On the support of χ̄, |V′ − 1| < (1 + κ_s)M₀ε_k. With C(M) ≥ 8(1 + κ_s)max(M₀, C_X(M)), V′ and e^{itX} each move the data by at most ε₁/8 per bond, so the data V′e^{itX}V′^A₀V_Λ, 0 ≤ t ≤ 1, is regular with 2ε₁, as in (c), "The comparison". At each V′ of the support the two runs' exponents therefore differ by at most the sum of two bounds.
    - The equal-data difference of g_k^{−2}A(ζ₀, U_{k,Z}(·)) at the data V′V′^A₀V_Λ. As for A(ζ₀, U_0) in (c), "The propagation", (b) bounds it by Σ_xC_w(ε₀)g_k^{−2}(ε/a_n)^{1−ε₀}ε₁² over the points x of the layers of 𝐁_k(Z). The weight has a_{j(x)}|∇ζ₀| ≤ C, since |∇ζ₀| ≤ C/a_k (*The weighted defect.*) and a_{j(x)} ≤ a_k.
    - The change of run B's g_k^{−2}A(ζ₀, U_{k,Z}(·)) from the data V′V′^A₀V_Λ to V′V′^B₀V_Λ. (c), "The propagation" and "The result", bounds it by C_A(M)g_k^{−2}(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁² per bond of Λ^{(k)}. That bound uses only that each data point contributes ≤ Cε₁|δD| at first order and ≤ C|δD|² at second order at regular data, with |δD| ≤ CΔ, so it holds at every V′ of the support and not only at V′ = 1.

    The Haar measure, δ_{G₀}, and χ̄ are the same in both runs at equal couplings, and the integrands are positive. So the Lemma of the Cauchy note's §8.6 (real backgrounds) gives |log d^B − log d^A| ≤ φ_d, with φ_d the sum of the two bounds. By the results of (b) and (c), φ_d is ≤ C_hθ̃^n per point of the layers of 𝐁_k(Z) plus C_hθ̃^n per bond of Λ^{(k)}. The decomposition (1.10), the bulk form f, the part −2⟨𝒜₂, J(1 − ζ₀)⟩, the linear term of (1.2), and V(ζ₀, ·) enter d only through the action A(ζ₀, U_{k,Z}(V′V_Λ)), so none of them is compared separately. On the complex class the complex version of the Lemma uses the lower bound of |d| by e^{−c(M)} times the integral of the modulus of its integrand, with e^{−c(M)} := exp(−O(1)|Λ|) = exp(−O(M⁴)) (p.358 l.24–30 with (1.10)–(1.11)). For a pointwise bound φ of the two runs' difference of the exponents it needs (e^φ − 1)e^{c(M)} ≤ ½, and it gives |log d^B − log d^A| ≤ 2(e^φ − 1)e^{c(M)} ≤ 4e^{c(M)}φ. The value D(1) at V′ = 1 of that difference does not depend on V′ and factors out of both integrals exactly, so φ is only the oscillation φ′_d := sup_{V′}|D(V′) − D(1)| over the support of χ̄. The data at V′ differs from that at V′ = 1 on Λ only, so by the kernel form of VAR Proposition 4 in (b) (rate ⅛δ₀) both runs' changes of U_{k,Z}, and their run difference, decay from Λ, and φ′_d ≤ C_d(M)C_hθ̃^n, with C_d(M) a constant of M that counts the points of the layers within the decay length of Λ, |Λ| = O(M⁴). The second bound above is already on the bonds of Λ^{(k)}. So (e^{φ′_d} − 1)e^{c(M)} ≤ ½ for n ≥ n_d(M), and then |log d^B − log d^A − D(1)| ≤ 4e^{c(M)}C_d(M)C_hθ̃^n, with |D(1)| ≤ φ_d. For n < n_d(M), each run's log d is bounded by Bałaban's bounds, and the factor θ̃^{−n_d} is absorbed in C_h. This uses the bounds of (b) and (c) on the class, which *The complex class.* derives under (T-c).
  - *The sum 𝐂_k.* LFR II collects the small terms of all the expansions of the Wilson actions, together with I(U_0) of (1.10), into 𝐂_k (p.369 l.2–7). 𝐂_k enters (1.49) as one term, and the expressions V(Y) of (1.68)–(1.69) through the re-summation of p.377 l.2–9. Among these small terms are the linear term of (1.20), the small form of (1.20) with its part −2⟨𝒜₂, J(1 − ζ)⟩, V(ζ, ·), the last three terms of (1.24), the pieces of (1.26)–(1.29), and A(ζ₁, U_0) of (1.31) (p.361 l.10–28, p.362 l.29–40, p.363 l.11–45, p.364 l.1–16). Since (1.49) is an equality for (1.1), 𝐂_k is given exactly by (1.37) (p.365 l.43–50, with the second term of its right-hand side included in O(1) by p.366 l.12), the localisation of p.367 l.50–p.368 l.4 with (1.48) (p.368 l.32–36), and (1.10). In the notation of (c), with (g″)^{−2} := 1/(g″_k(·))², and with the weights split as ζ₀ + (1 − ζ₀),

    𝐂_k = W_ext + g_k^{−2}A(ζ₀, U_k) − A((g″)^{−2}ζ₀, U″_k) − g_k^{−2}A(ζ₀, U_{k,Z}(V′_kV′₀V_Λ)) + A((g″)^{−2}ζ₁, U″_{h+2}(exp ig_kB, V″)) + ½Σ_{p∈T_η}η⁴ζ(x(p))|(DH″_{1,k,Z}B)(p)|² − log d + E_k(Λ),

    W_ext := A(w_e, U_k) − A(w_e, U″_k), w_e := (g″)^{−2}(1 − ζ₀).

    Here (g_k(·))^{−2} is g_k^{−2} on Z and (g″)^{−2} off Z (p.365 l.19), and the support of ζ₀ lies in Z (ζ_i ∈ C₀^∞(X_i), LFR I p.201 l.28, as p.365 l.33–40 uses it), so (g_k(·))^{−2}ζ₀ = g_k^{−2}ζ₀. On Z, (g″)^{−2} − g_k^{−2} is localised in Λ (p.359 l.36–39), and the support of 1 − ζ₀ is localised away from Λ, at a distance larger than 8MR_k from the support of B (p.363 l.43–44; the distance dist(Ω_k, Λ) of (1.6), p.357 l.33–38), so (g_k(·))^{−2}(1 − ζ₀) = w_e. Bałaban uses this identity himself: he combines the weights ((g″)^{−2} − g_k^{−2})(Z_k ∖ Z) and g_k^{−2}(1 − ζ₀) into (g″)^{−2}(1 − ζ₀) (p.364 l.36–45) and expands the result around U_k with the weight (g_k(·))^{−2}(1 − ζ₀) (p.365 l.29–34). The A(ζ₀, U_0) of (1.37) cancels against that of the denominator.
    - The part 𝐂_k − W_ext. At equal couplings E_k(Λ) is the same in both runs. Each of the four actions is a sum over the plaquettes in the support of its weight, which lies in Z, and its weight is of the kind treated in (b): g_k^{−2}ζ₀ as for d above, and (g″)^{−2}ζ₀ and (g″)^{−2}ζ₁ as the weight ζ₁/(g″)² of (1.70). The configurations are VAR minimisers with multiscale data. The shared variable is V_k, as in *The lines.*; then V′_k = V_k(V^X_Λ)^{−1} (p.378 l.12) depends on the run. U_k and U_{k,Z}(V′_kV′₀V_Λ) carry on Λ the data V′_kV′^X₀ (p.365 l.10–13), compared at equal V_k and regular as for d above, since χ̄ restricts V′_k in (1.1). U″_k carries the data exp(ig_kB)M^·(U^{(AL)}_0) of (1.50), and U″_{h+2}(exp ig_kB, V″) carries exp(ig_kB) and V″ only; both are regular as for the action of (1.70). So each action differs between the runs by ≤ C_hθ̃^n per point of the layers of its configuration in Z, by (b), plus ≤ C_hθ̃^n per data point of Λ, by (c). The form differs by ≤ ½r̄_nN_k ≤ ½C_hθ̃^n per point of 𝐁₀, by *The result for the forms.*, and log d by ≤ φ_d. Hence the two runs' values of 𝐂_k − W_ext differ by ≤ 11dC_hθ̃^n per point of the component's history: a point of a coarser layer of Z, a point of 𝐁₀, a data point, and a bond of Λ^{(k)} are each counted at a point of the history that contains it, and the factor d covers the bonds at a point. This uses the Cauchy note's (10.6c) through the form, apart from the form's open interface pieces, the point (6) of (LF-A), and *The complex class.* with (T-c) on the class, with the status of those items. It is derived at real configurations, and on the class by *The complex class.*, under (T-c).
    - *The shell of χ̄(Λ)(V′_k) (derived).* The first factor χ̄(Λ)(V′_k) of (1.1) depends on the run through V^X_Λ. It stands on the components of the second class only (p.378 l.10–16), and it is soft (§3(d), LFR I (1.101)).
      - At equal V_k the two runs' arguments f^X_b := |(1/i)log V′^X_k(b)| differ by at most the data difference s_n := C_Δ(M)(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁ per bond of (c), by the triangle inequality of the bi-invariant distance, since V′^B_k = V′^A_k·V^A_Λ(V^B_Λ)^{−1}.
      - The thresholds are t^X := M₀ε^X_k, with M₀ the same in both runs (§3(d), p.378 l.10–14) and |t^A − t^B| ≤ Ct^Ag_k²|δ_k| ≤ ½t^A (§2(b)).
      - Everywhere, χ̄(f/t) is Lipschitz in f with the constant 1/(κ_st), and |∂_tχ̄(f/t)| ≤ (1 + κ_s)/(κ_st), since the derivative vanishes where f ≥ (1 + κ_s)t. So per bond |χ̄(f^A_b/t^A) − χ̄(f^B_b/t^B)| ≤ δ^χ_n + δ^χ_c, with δ^χ_n := s_n/(κ_st^A) the data part and δ^χ_c := (1 + κ_s)|t^A − t^B|/(κ_s min(t^A, t^B)) ≤ 4Cg_k²|δ_k|/κ_s the coupling part.
      - The factor enters the split of §2(b) with the other tests of Π_X (§3(d), *The shell bound.*). Since (Π_A − Π_B)₊ ≤ 1{Π_A > 0}|Π_A − Π_B|, with every factor in [0, 1], and 1{Π_A > 0} is the product of run A's sharp tests at the thresholds (1 + κ_s)t^A for the passed tests, t^A for the failed ones, and the cascade thresholds of §3(d) (*The shell bound.*), telescoping over the bonds gives (Π_A − Π_B)₊ρ_A ≤ |Λ^{(k)}|(δ^χ_n + δ^χ_c) times run A's own term with these sharp tests, which (R-s) covers (§3(d)), and the same with A and B exchanged. So the shell is bounded as the shells of §3(d): by δ^χ_n + δ^χ_c per bond of Λ^{(k)}, times a run's own term with Bałaban's bounds. The coupling part is carried with |δ_k|, as in (b).
      - δ^χ_n ≤ C_χ(M)θ̃^n. Here ε₁/ε_k = C(M)R_k, with R_k ≤ L(log g_k^{−2})^r (CRE (2.5)), log g_k^{−2} ≤ log(ḡ^{−2} + b′(K − k) + C) (ONE_LOOP_PROOFS Lemma 2.1(i)), and K − k < 8n + j(C) (*The Ratio.* in (d)), so R_k is at most a power of log(ḡ^{−2} + 8bn + bj(C) + C). And (ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3} = L^{−(1−ε₀)n/4}L^{−(1−ε₀)n/12} ≤ θ̃^nL^{−(1−ε₀)n/12}, since θ̃ ≥ L^{−(1−ε₀)/4} ((c), the Cauchy note's §8.7). The slack L^{−(1−ε₀)n/12} absorbs R_k, and also the factor (k_s − k + 1)^{1/2} of ε₁/ε_k when §1's ε₁ is the larger one. So C_χ(M) depends on M, j(C), ḡ, L, ε₀, r, b, κ_s, and Bałaban's constants, and not on k, K, ε, or the volume.
    - *The linear term of (1.20).* g_k⟨DH″_{1,k,Z}B, ζη^{−2}Im∂U^0_{k,Z}⟩ is not compared term by term in this section. It enters only through 𝐂_k, so its run difference is inside the bound of 𝐂_k − W_ext above (derived), and a bound of its own, of the type of C_ℓ(M), is not needed. The same holds for the small form of (1.20) and V(ζ, ·). So, of *The result for the forms.*, the main proof uses only the bound of (1.21), which (1.49) and (1.70) keep: the bulk form f, the small form of (1.20), and the linear term of (1.2) lie inside d or 𝐂_k, and so do the remainders V(ζ₀, ·) and V(ζ, ·).
    - The couplings. Their differences enter 𝐂_k − W_ext in four ways. Through the weights they enter as the coupling enters the action in the Cauchy note's §8.4, ≤ Cε₁²(|δ_{j(x)}| + |δ_k|) per point. Through the data exp(ig_kB) at fixed B they enter by ≤ C(1 + β₀)²(k − j + 1)ε₁δ′_k|δ_k| per data point of the scale j, by (c), "The propagation", since |g^A_k − g^B_k||B| ≤ g_k²|δ_k|δ′_k on the support of χ′ and g_k²/g_j² ≤ (1 + β₀)²(k − j + 1) by CRE (2.6). Through log d and E_k(Λ) they enter as in the coupling items of the Cauchy note's §8.6. The form ½⟨DH″B, ζDH″B⟩ depends on them through its background U^0_{k,Z}, whose data contain exp(ig_kB); this is a data derivative of the same kind as the second, and it is bounded in the same way, per data point of the scale j.
    - *The Localised Pieces of 𝐂_k (open).* LFR II localises 𝐂_k with the old terms: the terms of the first part of (1.49), except the first two, are treated by (1.59) or (1.60) and the decoupling of σ₀, then by the representation (1.61)–(1.64) (p.373 l.10–17), and re-summed into V(X^∼) and V(Y) ((1.68), pp.376–377). So the comparison of each piece is that of *The functions of the configurations.* with the localised terms 𝐂(X) of 𝐂_k (p.367 l.50–p.368 l.4, (1.48)) in place of 𝐓(X), in three parts. (i) Their own run differences at a common configuration: the action pieces are sums over the plaquettes of X, bounded per point by (b) and (c) as in *The part 𝐂_k − W_ext.*, and the pieces of −log d are the localised terms of its expansion (p.358 l.24–30, (1.10)–(1.11)). For these, if each activity of a convergent polymer expansion changes between the runs by at most δ times its bound, δ ≤ ½, and the expansion converges at twice the activities, then each localised term of the logarithm is analytic in t on |t| < 1/δ along the activities z^A + t(z^B − z^A), bounded there by its bound M₂ at twice the activities, and Cauchy's estimate gives its run difference ≤ M₂δ/(1 − δ) ≤ 2δM₂; the relative change of the activities is the per-point bound of φ_d. (ii) The variation along the joint line of (ii) of that item, term by term with Bałaban's class bounds of 𝐂(X); these terms are of the generation k, so no age factor and no generation sum enter. (iii) The decoupled propagators, which need (W-δ). What is missing: the fine side and the current of the joint line (the open inputs of (ii) of that item), (W-δ), and the convergence of the expansion of log d at twice the activities, which a constant factor in Bałaban's smallness conditions would give but which is not checked against (1.10)–(1.11).
    - *The exterior part W_ext (derived).* U_k and U″_k have the same determining set and data off Λ (p.370 l.7–9), so W_ext is one weighted action at two configurations whose determining sets and data differ only on Λ (by (1.33)). Each of its two actions is a sum over the whole lattice, so (b) and (c) alone bound its run difference only by a sum over all points. The data on Λ is not close either. On the deep part Λ_1 := Λ ∖ Λ_0, Λ_0 := Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1} (LFR I p.195 l.27–29), U″_k carries the large-field data V″ of (1.50), and U_k carries the averages of a configuration that is small inside Λ. So no short segment of data joins the two. The comparison below localises each run's change near Λ through Bałaban's expansion of U″_k around U_k (p.370 l.10–p.371 l.49), sizes it by the per-block depth of (b), and combines the two bounds by min(u, v) ≤ u^{1/3}v^{2/3}. The decay budget is the following: the chain's fields decay at the rate δ₀/16 of the weight of (c), *The weighted bound.*; the power v^{2/3} keeps the rate δ₀/24; and the whole rate δ₀/24 goes into the count (2.58) at α = 1/24, which needs (2.59) at α = 1/24. It is at equal couplings, except in *The couplings of W_ext.* below, and in the common gauge of (c), "The gauge of the data", with U_0 in the AL-gauge from the beginning (LFR I p.197 l.42). Here ε₁ is the larger of the constants of §1 and (c), ε̂ := ε₁ + δ′_k, and w̄ := sup|w_e| ≤ g_k^{−2} + O(1)k (p.364 l.35–36). A point of Λ is a block of a layer of 𝐁″_k ∩ Λ or a bond of Λ^{(k)}; it is a point of the component's history at a layer ≥ n. A point of the history carries at most one block and d bonds, so a bound per point of Λ is at most d + 1 times the same bound per point of the history, a factor absorbed in C_h.
      - *The weight.* The weighted blocking artifact and the current of A(w_e, ·) need a_{j(x)}|∇w_e(x)| ≤ Cw̄, as in *The weighted defect.* For ζ₀ this is the choice made there, with a_{j(x)} ≤ a_k. At a point x of the layer m, (g″)^{−2}(x) − g_k^{−2} = β_m(1 − φ_m(x)) + Σ_{m<l≤k}β_l, as in the coupling-term item of (e), *The step map.*, with the layers of the sequence of domains of g″_k(·), whose bound O(1)(k − j) is p.364 l.35–36. The form of g″_k(·) through the functions φ_m is that of CRE (2.24). Here φ_m ∈ C_0^∞(Λ_m), φ_m = 1 on Λ_m^{∼−1}, as in CRE (2.24) (p.259 l.17–21). So only φ_m varies at x. Bałaban allows any such φ_m. The collar Λ_m ∖ Λ_m^{∼−1} is one layer of the cubes of which Λ_m is a union (CRE p.245 l.40–43), so its width is ≥ a_m, and φ_m is chosen, as ζ₀ and ζ₁ are, as a fixed smooth function with |∇φ_m| ≤ C/a_m, the same in both runs. F_K does not depend on the choice. By (2.24), −A(g_k^{−2}(·), U) − Σ_{j≤k}β_j(g_{j−1})A(φ_j, U) = −g_0^{−2}A(U) for every U, so φ_j only moves terms between the Wilson term of (2.23) and the counterterms of (2.25) (CRE p.253 l.25–37, p.258 l.49–51, p.259 l.13–27).
      - *The chain.* The domains of (1.50)–(1.55) are taken within Z, the union of the class of components (LFR I p.177 l.33, (1.1)); this is the reading (R-Z). There (Ω″_{h+1})^c, (Ω″^∼_{h+1})^c, and (Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1})^c stand for their intersections with Z, so U_2 and θ live in Z, and (1.53) puts U_1 on Ω″^∼_{h+1} ∪ Z^c and U_2 on (Ω″^∼_{h+1})^c ∩ Z. The same holds for the domains of the join in (1.52): Ω″^∼_{h+1} stands for Ω″^∼_{h+1} ∪ Z^c in 𝐁₁ and its complement for (Ω″^∼_{h+1})^c ∩ Z in 𝐁₂. It is Bałaban's convention for the section: "For simplicity we denote intersections of the regions Z_j with Z by Z_j also … We write the intersections explicitly only if it may lead to a misunderstanding" (LFR I p.177 l.33–35), and the operation 𝕋_k(Z_k ∩ Z^c), which the section does not change, is omitted from the formulas ((1.1), l.41–42). Three printed statements force it. By LFR I (1.12) (p.179 l.35) the literal (Ω″_{h+1})^c is Z″_{h+1} ∪ (Ω^c_{h+1} ∩ Z^c), and its enlargements' complements reach Z^c where Ω^c_{h+1} ∩ Z^c is deeper than one or two of their layers. So (1) and (3) fail in the literal reading whenever (Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1})^c ∩ Z^c ≠ ∅, and (2) whenever (Ω″^∼_{h+1})^c ∩ Z^c ≠ ∅. Such terms occur: the section does not restrict 𝕋_k(Z_k ∩ Z^c), and the components outside Z may be old and large. (1) U^0_k "considered on Z^c depends weakly on the field V″⌈(Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1})^c" (p.370 l.14–15), while on Z^c ⊂ Λ^c the field V″ is itself an argument of (1.50). (2) U_{1,2} "restricted to Z^c is equal to U_1" (p.371 l.26–27), while (1.53) puts U_2 on (Ω″^∼_{h+1})^c. (3) Λ ⊃ Z″_k ⊃ ⋯ ⊃ Z″_{h+1} ⊃ (Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1})^c (LFR I p.195 l.42), while Λ = (Ω^{∼4}_{k₀+1})^c ∩ Z ⊂ Z (LFR I (1.73), p.192 l.43), and already Z″_{h+1} ⊂ Z by (1.11). The reading leaves 𝐁″_k, U^0_k, U″_k, U_{k,Λ}, and U_k unchanged everywhere, since in (1.50) the literal overlap of Λ^c and (Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1})^c carries the same V″, so the data is the same in both readings. It changes the auxiliary objects 𝐁₁, 𝐁₂, U_1, U_2, U_{1,2}, and θ of (1.52)–(1.55) in Z^c. Off Z″_{h+1}, which contains Λ^c, U_1 carries the data V″⌈Λ^c of U^0_k and 𝐁₁ coincides with 𝐁″_k, since the boundary layer of the join (2.14) [III] (CRE p.257 l.15) lies where θ = 1, in Z″_{h+1} ⊂ Λ (p.371 l.2–4). So on supp w_e, U_{1,2} = U_1: on Z^c by (2), and in Z on Ω″^∼_{h+1} by (1.53) (p.370 l.26–33), whose complement in Z lies in (Ω″_{h+1})^c ∩ Z = Z″_{h+1} ⊂ Λ (LFR I p.179 l.35, p.195 l.42). The support of 1 − ζ₀ in Z is localised away from Λ, at the distance dist(Ω_k, Λ) of (1.6) (*The sum 𝐂_k.*), and Bałaban places it at a distance larger than 8MR_k from the support of B (p.363 l.43–44). So on supp w_e the four formulas (1.51), (1.54), (1.57), and (1.58) give U″_k from U_k in four links, each of the form U′ = (exp iη𝐇(Ψ)U)^{u^{−1}} (p.371 l.48–49). The arguments are the following.
        - (1.51): g_kB on 𝐁₀ ⊂ Λ, with |g_kB| < δ′_k by χ′ (p.360 l.41–43).
        - (1.54): 𝐆″_kJ_{1,2}, written by (1.55) (p.371 l.20–23) with the sources θJ_{1,2} and Q*_{𝐁₁}θ₁H*_{𝐁₁}J_1, which lie in Z″_{h+2} ⊂ Λ. Here θ ∈ C_0^∞((Ω″_{h+1})^c) (p.371 l.3), so by (R-Z) supp θ ⊂ (Ω″_{h+1})^c ∩ Z = Z″_{h+1} and θ = 0 on Z^c. θ₁, which is 1 where θ ≠ 0, 1 and decreases to 0 on a neighbourhood of that set (p.371 l.24), is chosen with support in Z″_{h+1} as well; Bałaban's conditions fix θ₁ only on that set and its neighbourhood, so this is an admissible choice. For (1.55) to be an identity, θ₁ = 1 on the block bonds c whose blocks B(c_±) meet {0 < θ < 1}, so the neighbourhood is at least one block of the scale ≤ h wide. It fits in Z″_{h+1}: θ is taken with its transition a few h-blocks inside (Ω″_{h+1})^c, which the strip between the layer of width 2M₁ and ∂Ω″_{h+1}, of width (LMR_{h+1} − 2M₁)L^{−(k−h)}, allows (M₁ < M, CRE p.245 l.35–37). The blocks of 𝐁₁ that meet Z″_{h+1} are of the scales ≤ h, by LFR I (1.13) and (2.14) [III], and Q* sends the datum of a block bond c into the two blocks B(c_−) and B(c_+) that it joins (CMP 98 (14), p.19 l.38–42); the multiscale Q_{𝐁₁} is the composition of these one-step averages, whose contours nest inside the two blocks. So Q*_{𝐁₁}θ₁H*_{𝐁₁}J_1 lies within 2√dL^{−(k−h)} of Z″_{h+1}. For h + 2 ≤ k − N₀, which holds since N ≫ N₀, the sets are (Ω^{∼5}_j)^c ∩ Z by (1.11), and since {Ω″_j} is an admissible sequence (LFR I p.179 l.40), Z″_{h+1} and Z″^c_{h+2} are separated by more than RM in the units of the scale h + 1 (PRT2 (2.2), p.224 l.9–14), the pattern of the widths of p.179 l.19–24. This exceeds 2√dL^{−(k−h)}, since 2√d < LRM for M large (CRE p.245 l.34–37). So this source lies in Z″_{h+2}, and Z″_{h+2} ⊂ Λ by p.195 l.42. The currents obey a_{j(x)}³|J_{1,2}(x)| ≤ 4(d − 1)C₀ε₁, since U_{1,2} is regular at its layers (p.370 l.35–38) and |J| ≤ 2(d − 1)|∇F| as in the current term above. The second argument is H″_{1,k}Q̃(η𝐀₀) on Z″_k ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1}, with |𝐀₀| < O(1)B₃²B₅M⁶ε_k by LFR I (1.87) (p.197 l.33–35).
        - (1.57): −(1/i)log M^·(U_{k,Λ}) on Z″_k, bounded by LFR I (1.84) (p.371 l.32–33).
        - (1.58): −(1/i)log V′_k on Λ, < (1 + κ_s)M₀ε_k on the support of the soft χ̄(Λ) (LFR I (1.101)).

        With C(M) ≥ O(1)B₃²B₅M⁶ + M₀, every argument is ≤ C_Ψε̂ in the dimensionless norm of its layer. All of them lie in Λ except 𝐆″_kJ_{1,2}, whose sources lie in Λ and which decays from it: 𝐆″_k is the operator 𝔊 of PBG (3.148) for 𝐁″_k (p.370 l.48–49), whose kernel has the bounds (3.42) with the decay e^{−δ₀d(y,y′)} by Theorem 3.13 (PBG p.426 l.29–32, p.397 l.37–p.398 l.25), so |𝐆″_kJ_{1,2}| ≤ C_Ψε̂e^{−δ₀d(·,Λ)}, faster than the weight e^{(δ₀/16)d(·,Λ)} below. Here C_Ψ is a constant of Bałaban's constants, for 𝐆″_kJ_{1,2} through the bounds and the localisation of the kernel of 𝐆″_k, which Bałaban uses for (1.55) (p.370 l.46–49, p.371 l.24–25). Also ε̂ ≤ 2ε₁, since O(1)δ′_k < ε_k with δ′_k = g_kA₁p₁(g_k) (LFR I p.183 l.2, p.184 l.18). Each 𝐇 is the function of VAR Sects. D, E for its determining set (p.370 l.38–39), analytic with the derivative bounds VAR (190) (p.308 l.37–47), which Bałaban uses for it (p.360 l.20–22). Its defining contraction closes in the weighted norm with the weight e^{(δ₀/16)d(·,Λ)}, by the kernel form of VAR Proposition 4 of (b), as in (c), *The weighted bound.* So a_{j(x)}|𝐇(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇𝐇(x)| ≤ C_𝐇ε̂e^{−(δ₀/16)d(y,Λ)} at x ∈ Δ(y), with C_𝐇 a constant of M and Bałaban's constants, given by C_Ψ and the constants of the bounds of 𝔊 and ℋ in (c), *The weighted bound.* (C_𝐇 is not the C_ℋ of (c)). The configurations of the chain lie in the regular class ((1.65), p.375 l.17–35), and the gauge transformations u leave the plaquette actions unchanged.
      - *Run B's chain at run A's data.* The four formulas are identities of each run's minimisation maps. They use U_0 only through its averages and the bounds (1.84) and (1.87). So they hold, with the same bounds, for run B's maps at the data built from run A's U^A_0. Let U^X_k[Y] be run X's U_{𝐁_k} at the data V′_kV_Λ, with V_Λ = M^k(U^Y_0) on Λ^{(k)}. Let U″^X_k[Y] be run X's U_{𝐁″_k} at the data exp(ig_kB)M^·(U^Y_0) on Λ_0 and the shared data elsewhere. Let W^X[Y] be the corresponding exterior part. Then W^B_ext − W^A_ext = (W^B[B] − W^B[A]) + (W^B[A] − W^A[A]).
      - *The data part W^B[B] − W^B[A].* In both actions the two runs' data differ only on Λ. For U_k the difference is e^{iX} on Λ^{(k)}, with ‖X‖_∞ ≤ Δ ≤ C_Δ(M)(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁ by (c). For U″_k it is the data difference of (c), ≤ C′_Δ(M)(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁ per data point of Λ_0. On Λ_1 both carry V″. Along the segments of (c), "The comparison", the data stays regular with 2ε₁. The data derivative of run B's A(w_e, ·) at a unit datum e_z is ⟨H^B_1e_z, J^B(w_e)⟩. By *The weight.*, |J^B(w_e)| ≤ 2c_ww̄ε₁a_j^{−3} on the segments, as for J^A(ζ₀) in the current term, with the factor 2 for the regularity 2ε₁. The lift obeys a_j|H^B_1e_z| ≤ 2B₀K(y, z) on Δ(y), as for 𝒜_z in the linear term of (1.2), and the column sums of K are ≤ c_K. So the derivative is ≤ 4dB₀c_Kc_ww̄ε₁ per unit datum, and the fundamental theorem of calculus along the segments gives |W^B[B] − W^B[A]| ≤ 4dB₀c_Kc_w(C_Δ(M) + C′_Δ(M))w̄ε₁²(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3} per point of Λ.
      - *The equal-data part W^B[A] − W^A[A].* It is Σ_yω_y over the blocks y of the layers that meet supp w_e. Here ω_y := [a^B_y(U^B_k[A]) − a^A_y(U^A_k[A])] − [a^B_y(U″^B_k[A]) − a^A_y(U″^A_k[A])], with a^X_y run X's weighted plaquette action of the block (run B's through the weights w_{p,p′} of §9.3). It has two bounds.
        - (u) Each bracket is the per-block run difference at equal data of (b): b_{w_e}(U^B) at y plus a_y(w_e, MU^B) − a_y(w_e, U^A). The block density has first derivative ≤ Cw̄ε₁ per unit dimensionless field, so with the profile ρ̄(y) of (b) and the weighted blocking artifact of *The weighted defect.*, |ω_y| ≤ u_y := C_ew̄(ε/a_{j(y)})^{1−ε₀}ε₁², with C_e := C(2C_G(ε₀)c″_K + C_b + C_ζ).
        - (v) Grouped by runs instead, |ω_y| ≤ Σ_X|a^X_y(U^X_k[A]) − a^X_y(U″^X_k[A])|. Each link of the chain changes the block density by ≤ C_Lw̄(2ε₁ + |𝐇|)|𝐇|, as in (c), "The comparison". With two runs and four links, |ω_y| ≤ v_y := 8C_L(2 + C_𝐇)C_𝐇w̄ε̂²e^{−(δ₀/16)d(y,Λ)}.

        Hence |ω_y| ≤ u_y^{1/3}v_y^{2/3} ≤ C′w̄ε̂²(ε/a_{j(y)})^{(1−ε₀)/3}Σ_{z∈Λ}e^{−(δ₀/24)d(y,z)}, with C′ := C_e^{1/3}(8C_L(2 + C_𝐇)C_𝐇)^{2/3}, since ε₁ ≤ ε̂ and e^{−(δ₀/24)d(y,Λ)} ≤ Σ_{z∈Λ}e^{−(δ₀/24)d(y,z)}. For a point z of Λ, the count (2.58) at α = 1/24 bounds Σ_y(ε/a_{j(y)})^{(1−ε₀)/3}e^{−(δ₀/24)d(y,z)} by c‴(ε/a_{j(z)})^{(1−ε₀)/3}, with c‴ := 6c₀^d(1/48)(14 + 13L^{1/3}). This uses Bałaban's condition (2.59) at α = 1/24, (1/96)δ₀RM > 2d log c₀(1/48) + 1, and ⅛δ₀RM ≥ 4 log L + 1 of (b), which gives (1/96)δ₀RM ≥ (1/3)log L + 1/12. By (2.58) a layer r steps from the layer of z contributes ≤ 6c₀^d(1/48)e^{−(1/96)δ₀RM(r−1)_+} times the ratio of the factors (ε/a_j)^{(1−ε₀)/3}.
        - The layer of z contributes ≤ 6c₀^d(1/48).
        - A layer m steps finer carries the ratio ≤ L^{m/3} and contributes ≤ 6c₀^d(1/48)L^{m/3}e^{−((1/3)log L + 1/12)(m−1)} = 6c₀^d(1/48)L^{1/3}e^{−(m−1)/12}. This pays for the layers of other components older than n.
        - A layer m steps coarser contributes ≤ 6c₀^d(1/48)e^{−(m−1)/12}.
        - Σ_{m≥1}e^{−(m−1)/12} = (1 − e^{−1/12})^{−1} < 13.

        Since j(z) ≥ n, the equal-data part is ≤ c‴C′w̄ε̂²(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3} per point of Λ.
      - *The result.* At equal couplings, |W^B_ext − W^A_ext| ≤ C_E(M)w̄ε̂²(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3} per point of Λ, with C_E(M) := 4dB₀c_Kc_w(C_Δ(M) + C′_Δ(M)) + c‴C′. Here w̄ ≤ (1 + O(1)k)g_k^{−2} and ε̂ ≤ 2ε₁. If (c)'s constant is the larger, ε₁ = C(M)R_kε_k and w̄ε̂² ≤ 4(1 + O(1)k)C(M)²R_k²p₀(g_k)², by ε_k = g_kp₀(g_k) (CRE (2.4), p.255 l.33). If §1's constant is the larger, ε₁/ε_k is at most C(1 + β₀)(k_s − k + 1)^{1/2}(1 + A₁/A₀) by CRE (2.6), and k_s − k < 7n, so ε₁² gains at most a factor linear in n. As in the result of (c), this is a power of log(k + j(C) + ḡ^{−2}) times a polynomial in n (k < 8n + j(C), the Cauchy note's §8.6), independent of K, and L^{−(1−ε₀)n/3} ≤ θ̃^nL^{−(1−ε₀)n/12} absorbs it. So the change is ≤ C_hθ̃^n per point of Λ, with C_h a constant of M, ε₀, ḡ, j(C), and Bałaban's constants, taken at least as large as C_E(M) requires. The points of Λ are points of the component's history, so this is the form (LF-2) of the Cauchy note's §8.6. With *The part 𝐂_k − W_ext.*, the two runs' values of 𝐂_k differ by ≤ (11d + 1)C_hθ̃^n per point of the component's history. The bound uses neither 𝒜₂ nor a remainder. It is derived at real configurations, and on the class in the last item.
      - *The couplings of W_ext.* With the two runs' couplings, W^B_ext − W^A_ext = [A(w^B_e − w^A_e, U^B_k) − A(w^B_e − w^A_e, U″^B_k)] + [W^B_ext(w^A_e) − W^A_ext(w^A_e)], with W^B_ext(w^A_e) run B's exterior part with run A's weight. The second bracket is the comparison above, except that run B's data contains exp(ig^B_kB) on 𝐁₀ ⊂ Λ_0. That is a data difference of ≤ g_k²|δ_k|δ′_k per bond, as in *The couplings.* above, and the data part bounds it by 4dB₀c_Kc_ww̄g_k²ε₁δ′_k|δ_k| per point of Λ, with w̄g_k² ≤ 1 + O(1)kg_k² (p.364 l.35–36). This is of the form of the data term of *The couplings.* The first bracket keeps the weight difference under the sum over the blocks.
        - At a block y of the layer m, w^B_e − w^A_e = (φ_mδ_m + (1 − φ_m)δ_{m−1})(1 − ζ₀) by *The weight.*, with δ_m the two runs' difference of g_m^{−2} of the Cauchy note's §8.3. So |w^B_e − w^A_e| ≤ ω_m := |δ_m| + |δ_{m−1}| and a_m|∇(w^B_e − w^A_e)| ≤ Cω_m on Δ(y), with the C of *The weight.* So (v), for one run and with ω_m in place of w̄, bounds the block's part by 4C_L(2 + C_𝐇)C_𝐇ω_mε̂²e^{−(δ₀/16)d(y,Λ)}.
        - With e^{−(δ₀/16)d(y,Λ)} ≤ Σ_{z∈Λ}e^{−(δ₀/24)d(y,z)} and the count (2.58) at α = 1/24 of the equal-data part, a layer r steps from the layer of z contributes ≤ 6c₀^d(1/48)q^{(r−1)_+} times its ω_m, with q := L^{−1/3}e^{−1/12}.
        - The near layers m ≥ n contribute ≤ 2·6c₀^d(1/48)(1 + 2·13)max_{n−1≤l≤k}|δ_l|, since Σ_{r≥1}q^{r−1} < 13.
        - The far layers m ≤ n − 1 lie r ≥ n − m steps from z, since j(z) ≥ n. There |δ_m| and |δ_{m−1}| are ≤ |δ_{n−1}| + C_βΣ_{i=m}^{n−1}d_i, since δ_{m−1} − δ_{n−1} = Σ_{i=m}^{n−1}Δβ_i and |Δβ_i| ≤ C_βd_i (the Cauchy note's §8.3). With Σ_{m≤i}q^{n−1−m} ≤ 13q^{n−1−i}, they contribute ≤ 2·78c₀^d(1/48)(|δ_{n−1}| + C_βD^♭_{n−1}), with D^♭_l := Σ_{i≤l}q^{l−i}d_i.

        Hence the first bracket is ≤ C_wcε̂²[max_{n−1≤l≤k}|δ_l| + C_βD^♭_{n−1}] per point of Λ, with C_wc := 1920C_L(2 + C_𝐇)C_𝐇c₀^d(1/48), and C_wcε̂² ≤ 1 for γ small, since ε̂ ≤ 2ε₁. The Cauchy note's recursion accepts this form. Each |δ_l| with n − 1 ≤ l ≤ k is the coupling part of 𝒟_l, and 𝒟_l ≤ e^{½C_P(k−l)}𝒟_{[k]} with k − l + 1 ≤ P_k(Z), as for the exponent differences on a component in the Cauchy note's §8.6. D^♭_{n−1} is D_{n−1} with the rate q in place of L^{−β′}, and D_l ≤ D^♭_l since β′ > 1/3. In the weighted norm of the Cauchy note's §8.7, Σ_{i≤l}q^{l−i}m_i ≤ (1 − L^{−1/3}/θ̃)^{−1}m_l, since q < L^{−1/3} < L^{−(1−ε₀)/4} ≤ θ̃; this is the bound that §8.7 uses for D_l[u], with (1 − L^{−1/3}/θ̃)^{−1} in place of (1 − L^{−β′}/θ̃)^{−1}. A bound of the far layers at the rate L^{−β′} does not work, since the weight's rate δ₀/16 gives only q per layer. The bound is derived at real configurations, and the class item below carries it to the class.
      - *The complex class of W_ext.* The bound holds on the class, through the Lemma (Two Constants) on the lines of *The complex class.*, under (T-c).
        - At the real points of a line the configurations are real, with data regular with ε₁′, and the shared variables B, V″, and V_k are fixed. The chain's arguments move with the line by at most the moves of *The real points.*, which ε′_k contains. So the derivation above holds with ε₁′ for ε₁ and ε̂′ := ε₁′ + δ′_k for ε̂, and the run difference of W_ext is ≤ 𝔪 := C_E(M)w̄ε̂′²(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3} per point of Λ.
        - On the disc |z| < 25 the line lies in the analyticity domains of VAR Sect. G (*The disc.*). So the four formulas, identities between analytic functions at the real points, hold there. Their arguments are ≤ 26C_Ψε̂′, so the weighted contraction of *The chain.*, which holds at the complex arguments of these domains, gives a_{j(x)}|𝐇(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇𝐇(x)| ≤ 26C_𝐇ε̂′e^{−(δ₀/16)d(y,Λ)} at x ∈ Δ(y). The plaquettes are ≤ 52C₀ε₁′/a_j², the regularity 2ε₁ of (v) with 26ε₁′ for ε₁. So each link changes the block density by ≤ C_Lw̄(52ε₁′ + 26C_𝐇ε̂′)26C_𝐇ε̂′e^{−(δ₀/16)d(y,Λ)} ≤ 26²C_L(2 + C_𝐇)C_𝐇w̄ε̂′²e^{−(δ₀/16)d(y,Λ)}, which is (v) for one run at its own data with the factor 26². With e^{−(δ₀/16)d(y,Λ)} ≤ Σ_{z∈Λ}e^{−(δ₀/24)d(y,z)} and Σ_ye^{−(δ₀/24)d(y,z)} ≤ c_tot(1/24) (the count of (b), under (2.59) at α = 1/24), each run's W_ext, over four links, is ≤ ½𝔐 per point of Λ, with 𝔐 := 8·26²C_L(2 + C_𝐇)C_𝐇c_tot(1/24)w̄ε̂′².
        - The Lemma, applied to the run difference along the line divided by the number of points of Λ, gives at z = i the bound 𝔪^{15/16}𝔐^{1/16} ≤ max(C_E(M), 8·26²C_L(2 + C_𝐇)C_𝐇c_tot(1/24))w̄ε̂′²(ε/a_n)^{5(1−ε₀)/16} per point of Λ. Since ε̂′ ≤ 2ε₁′, w̄ε̂′² carries the factors of g_{j(x)}^{−2}ε₁′², and "The absorption" of *The results.* gives ≤ C_hθ̃^n per point of Λ on the class, since 5/16 exceeds 1/4.
        - The couplings enter as in *The couplings of W_ext.* The first bracket there is linear in the weight difference, and z = i lies in the disc, so the disc bound of the preceding sub-item, with ω_m in place of w̄ and one run, bounds it on the class by 26²C_wcε̂′²[max_{n−1≤l≤k}|δ_l| + C_βD^♭_{n−1}] per point of Λ, with the same count. The data term exp(ig_kB) is bounded by Cauchy's estimate on the data disc of (c), "The remainder".
- *The result for the forms.* With the alignment, per point of 𝐁₀ (for (1.21)) and of Λ^{(k)} (for the bulk form f of (1.2)), the two runs' forms differ by ≤ r̄_nN_k, apart from the two open pieces named below. Here r̄_n := C_F(M)(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3} and C_F(M) := C_d(2 + C_ζ) + (1 + c_bf)(2x̂_E + x̂_E²) + c_4c_alε₁, with c_4 := 1 + 2(1 + d²)(C₀ + 2α₁) + 4dB₀c_KC₂α_J. On the alignment's disc the lifts obey (3.133) (PBG Theorems 3.4, p.400, and 3.12), |F| ≤ (2C₀ε₁ + 4α₁)/a_j², and |𝐉| ≤ α_Ja_j^{−3}, so the forms are ≤ c_4N_k per point there. The bound holds since a_j ≥ a_n, ε₁ ≤ 1, and γ_d, 1, and 2(1 − ε₀)/3 all are at least (1 − ε₀)/3. The k-dependent ε₁ = C(M)R_kε_k enters C_F(M) only through ε₁ ≤ 1 and the conditions of (T-Λ). The terms in E use the Cauchy note's (10.6c) in its multiscale form. The two open pieces are the π-part of β for multiscale data, whose missing interface bound *The terms in E.* states, and, for the bulk form f of (1.2), the twist term 2⟨B̃₂, ν_0⟩ where Λ meets an interface, whose missing terms the (1.2)-form bullet of *The weighted defect.* states. Apart from these, the bound is derived at real configurations, and on the class *The complex class.* gives it with max(C_F(M), 2c_4)(ε/a_n)^{5(1−ε₀)/16} in place of r̄_n. The other parts of the exponents of (1.2) and (1.20) are as follows.
  - The small form of (1.20) is the quadratic form of (1.20) less (1.21). The identity of the (1.2)-form bullet holds with the weight ζ, the set 𝐁″_k(Z), and its multiplier λ″: U^0_{k,Z} is critical for the unweighted problem with all of its data, so J_0 = 𝐐″*λ″ = Q″*ν″, with Q″ the multiscale double bar and ν″ = (1 − Ξ)*λ″ by *The interface twist.*. In VAR's gauge Q″𝒜₂ + C″^{(2)}[𝒜₁, 𝒜₁] = 0 everywhere, since the twisted data is a function of the plain data and is fixed with it, so no twist term enters. So the small form is ⟨𝒜₁, Δ′(ζ)𝒜₁⟩ − 2⟨C″^{(2)}[𝒜₁, 𝒜₁], ν″⟩ − 2⟨𝒜₂, J(1 − ζ)⟩, with Δ′(ζ) := Δ^{act}(ζ) − D*ζD the curvature terms of PBG (3.10). The first two terms are f with ζ in place of ζ₀, less (1.21). Their defect is β_f − β_w, which consists of the curvature couplings, the curvature piece of the weight-gradient sum, and the current term, all within the per-cell bound above, and their terms in E are the curvature and constraint terms of f^A, bounded with c_bf. So they differ between the runs by ≤ r̄_nN_k per point of 𝐁₀, apart from the open π-part. The third term is the part carried by the weight 1 − ζ, as for (1.2). The small form enters the main proof only through 𝐂_k, compared as a whole in *The complementary-weight parts.*, so the third term is not compared separately.
  - The third-order remainders V(ζ, ·) and V(ζ₀, ·) enter the main proof only through 𝐂_k and d, compared as wholes in *The complementary-weight parts.*, so they are not bounded separately.
  - The linear term of (1.2). In each run it equals (1/g_k)⟨𝒜₁, J(1 − ζ₀)⟩, by (1.4)–(1.5). It is linear in B′, and for a unit datum e_z at z ∈ Λ^{(k)} the lift 𝒜_z := H₁(1 + Ξ)e_z has a_j|𝒜_z| + a_j²|∇𝒜_z| ≤ 2B₀K(y, z) on Δ(y), as in the bound of 𝒜 above, with B₀ standing in this bullet for c_zB₀, c_z := 1 + 4de^{(d+1)δ₀}: (1 + Ξ)e_z has at most 2d + 1 nonzero entries, each ≤ 2, within (d + 1)a_j of z, by *The interface twist.*. The alignment moves run A's term to the pair (Ũ, J′). In divergence form (the Cauchy note's §10.3), with the plaquettes of the alignment's disc ≤ 2α₁η²/a_j² and |D𝒜_z| ≤ 6B₀K(y, z)a_j^{−2}, the term is ≤ 6d²c_Wα₁B₀c_K/g_k per unit datum on the disc, so the alignment costs at most c_al(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁ times this. At the pair, with H^Ae_z = Q₁𝒜_z − E_z, the difference is (1/g_k)(db_{1−ζ₀}(U^B_0)[𝒜_z] − ⟨E_z, J^A(1 − ζ₀)⟩).
    - The first piece is the Cauchy note's (9.5) at the scale j, with the weight 1 − ζ₀ on each coarse term, plus the first variation −Σ_pε⁴Σ_{p′}w_{p,p′}(w(p′) − w(p))⟨F_{p′}, (D𝒜_z)(p′)⟩ of the weight-gradient sum. On Δ(y) it is ≤ d²[C_r(ε/a_j) + C₀C_ζ(ε/a_n)]ε₁·2B₀K(y, z).
    - The second piece is ≤ dc_wε₁a_j|E_z| on Δ(y), by |J^A(1 − ζ₀)| ≤ c_wε₁a_j^{−3} as for J^A(ζ₀). The sources of E_z are sized by 𝒜_z, so a_j|E_z| ≤ (C_G^Q(ε₀)C_d + 12d²L²C_U(M))(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}Σ_{y′}K(y, y′)2B₀K(y′, z), as in the bound of E above, apart from the open π-part.
    - The sum over y uses the column sums of K, which the count of (b) bounds by c_K as it bounds the row sums, the factor L^{2n} falling on the other side. Per unit datum the difference is ≤ (2B₀c_Kε₁/g_k)[d²(C_r + C₀C_ζ) + dc_wx̂_E + 3d²c_Wα₁c_al](ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}. With |B′| ≤ b_k and ε₁/g_k = C(M)R_kp₀(g_k) (CRE (2.4)), the linear term changes per point of Λ^{(k)} by ≤ C_ℓ(M)R_kp₀(g_k)b_k(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}, with C_ℓ(M) := 2B₀c_KC(M)[d²(C_r + C₀C_ζ) + dc_wx̂_E + 3d²c_Wα₁c_al]. The E-piece uses the Cauchy note's (10.6c) in its multiscale form, with the π-part open as in *The terms in E.*.

  Per point, r̄_nN_k = 2d²(2B₀c_K(1 + 2dL))²C_F(M)b_k²(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}. Since b_k, R_k, and p₀(g_k) are powers of log g_k^{−2} ≤ log(ḡ^{−2} + b′(K − k) + C) (ONE_LOOP_PROOFS Lemma 2.1(i)), up to the factor L in R_k (CRE (2.5)), and k, K − k < 8n + j(C), the changes of the forms, of the bulk part of the small form, and of the linear term are ≤ C_hθ̃^n per point, since θ̃ ≥ L^{−(1−ε₀)/4}, exactly as in the result of (c). Bałaban's bounds of the open parts are O(1)B₃⁵B₅M⁵A₁²p₁²(g_k)ε_k|𝐁₀| for the small form (p.361 l.20), O(1)|𝐁₀|δ′_k³/g_k² for V(ζ, ·) (p.361 l.11–13), and O(g_k^{1−β})|Λ| for V(ζ₀, ·) (p.357 l.9–10).
- *The functions of the configurations.* V(Y) of (1.68)–(1.69) re-sums the old terms 𝐓 ∈ {𝐄^{(j)}, 𝐑^{(j)}, 𝐁^{(j)}} at the configurations U″_k, U^0_k, U_{1,2}, U_1, U_{k,Λ}, and U_k. These configurations are linked by (1.51), (1.54), (1.57), (1.58), and (1.61)–(1.64) (pp.370–375). The re-summation uses the identities (1.59)–(1.60) and the decoupling expansion of RG II (1.9)–(1.10), which is the fundamental theorem of calculus in the parameters s(Δ) (RG II p.4 l.2–35). LFR II cites RG II's §§1–2 as "Sects. 6, 7 [I]", and its (1.9)–(1.10) and (1.23)–(1.29) as "(6.9)–(6.10), (6.24)–(6.29) [I]" (p.372 l.16–19, p.376 l.42); RG I has §§0–5 only. Each term of (1.10) is the Cauchy integral (1.23) of one old term and is bounded through that term's bound alone (RG II (1.24)–(1.29), pp.7–8). Here 𝐓 stands for the renormalised combinations, 𝐄^{(j)}(Λ_j, ·, z) − 𝐄^{(j)}(Λ_j, 1, z) − β_jA(h_z, ·) at the points z and 𝐑^{(j)}(X, ·) − 𝐑^{(j)}(X, 1) (CRE (2.43)–(2.44)), since for these terms the re-summation "includes renormalization in the form described in the proof of Theorem 2" (p.376 l.40–41). The completions that p.377 l.22–26 adds and subtracts are single terms of the new action at U_k, so the split below applies to them at that one configuration. With them, Σ_YV(Y) is Σ_X[𝐓(X, U″_k) − 𝐓(X, U_k)] re-expanded. The re-summation is linear in the old terms, but the operator that re-sums them depends on the run: each term of (1.10) evaluates an old term at the run's decoupled fluctuation field, built from that run's propagators H(s), G̃(s), and H₀(s) at complex s (RG II p.3 l.30–37, (1.23)). So the two runs' V(Y), which (1.68) (p.377 l.10–14) bounds with the weight exp(−(1 + 2β)κd_{k,Z}(Y)), differ by the parts (i) and (ii) below at the chain's configurations and by the part (iii) of the decoupled propagators. The contour of the parameter t_□ in (1.23) is the circle 1/|t_□| = 8B₀C₁e^{16κ₁}α₂^{−1}g_k|B| of RG II (1.22) (p.7). It is a function of g_k|B| and Bałaban's constants, so at equal B and g_k the two runs use the same circle. The derivative at t_□ = 0 that (1.23) represents does not depend on the radius, so where the runs' g_k differ the smaller radius serves both, with the larger 1/|t_□| in (1.24).
  - (i) The term's own difference, at run A's configurations. Let G(X, ·) := 𝐓^B(X, (ι_X(·), Q₁^{pl}(ι_X·)*J(·))) be run B's term at the pair lift of the Cauchy note's §3, with J(·) run A's current (I.1.8), Q₁^{pl}(W) := dM at W, and ι_X the localised lift of the Cauchy note's §9.8 on the localisation domain X of the term. G is a functional of configurations on T_ε, and for 𝐓 = 𝐄^{(j)}, G − 𝐓^A is the frozen difference Δ_j of (α).
    - For the renormalised combinations G is renormalised with its own coefficient Π^G_{22,11} and its plane-resolved part N_j, as in the Cauchy note's §3, so G − 𝐓^A is irr(Δ_j) with Π^Δ_{22,11} = Π^G_{22,11} − β^A_j.
    - The comparison (ii) evaluates all terms of V(Y) at one lift, ι_Y, the localised lift of the Cauchy note's §9.8 with Y^∼ in place of X^∼ (its tail at the multiscale chain open, Open Gaps (3)). The counterterm of a term at z is the weighted action A(h_z, ·), and supp h_z lies in the scale-j cubes at z and its neighbours, so inside X^∼ ⊂ Y^∼. Since Mι_YU = U on Y^∼ and ι_Y1 = 1, run B's renormalised term at the pair lift through ι_Y differs from the renormalised G by
      r_z := (Π^G_{22,11} − β^B_{j+1})A_ε(h_z, c^A) + N_j(c^A) + β^B_{j+1}b_{h_z}(ι_Yc^A) + ℓ_X(c^A).
      Here b_{h_z}(W) := A_ε(h_z, MW) − A_{ε/L}(h_z, W) is the weighted blocking artifact, and ℓ_X(U) := 𝐓^B(X, (ι_YU, Q₁^{pl}(ι_YU)*J(U))) − G(X, U) is the lift change, which vanishes at U = 1 and is governed by the tail of ι_X and ι_Y on X (the Cauchy note's §9.8). The first three parts are per point z and ℓ_X is per domain X, so as a per-X identity it needs the assignment of the counterterm discussed below. For the 𝐑-terms, which carry no counterterm, r_z = ℓ_X(c^A).
    - At the composites of (1.59) and at the representation configurations of (1.61)–(1.63), r_z is part of the variation groups (iv) and (α) below, which take run B's own minimisers as the partner: (iv) = [renB(U^B) − renB(ι_YU^A)] + r_z, and it is bounded with them by the geometric-mean bound of the Cauchy note's (5.1v). The completions of p.377 l.22–26, single terms at U_k, are terms of V(Y), and U_k is a member of 𝒞, so V^B(Y)[c] reads them at the entry ι_YU^A_k, not at run B's own U^B_k. Their r_z at U_k is part of the open item below.
    - **Open: r_z at the members of 𝒞 read inside V(Y).** There the partner of renG(X, c^A) is run B's term at ι_Yc^A, which is not a local minimiser of run B, so (5.1c) gives no age bound at it and the device of (5.1v) does not apply. Without it the moment defect, its plane-resolved part N_j included, is, per scale-j point, at most 2496C_XC₀²E₀C_Π(j)ε₁²L^{4(j−n)}e^{−δ₀M₁L^j}, with no age factor. Summed over the L^{4(n−j)} scale-j points of a layer-n point and over j ≤ n it is at most 2496C_XC₀²E₀Σ_{j≤n}C_Π(j)ε₁²e^{−δ₀M₁L^j} ≤ 2496C_XC₀²E₀Σ_jmax(1, k − j)e^{−δ₀M₁L^j} per point, by C_Π(j)ε₁² ≤ max(1, k − j) (the Cauchy note's *The moment of the lifted term.*): O(k)E₀ at worst, and not C_hθ̃^n. The lift change, bounded by Cauchy's estimate at the tail, is per scale-j point of the size C_XE₀(ε₁/r_{0,j})e^{−δ₀M₁L^j/2}, without the factor L^{4(j−n)}, so the count makes it worse. The blocking artifact is small only after the sum over z at one configuration, Σ_zb_{h_z}(W) = b_1(W), which is ≤ 16C_b(ε/a_n)ε₁² per point per generation by (9.3)–(9.4) of the Cauchy note; its weight-change part, 768C₀²(ε/a_j)ε₁² per point per generation, cancels only in that sum. That sum crosses the domains Y, so it is not available per Y. The counterterm A(h_z, ·) belongs to the renormalised sum over the domains X ∋ z at the point z, not to one X, and its assignment among the domains Y into which these X are re-summed is not stated. What closes it:
      - (a) an age bound of run B's renormalised terms at the lifted chain ι_Yc^A together with (5.1c) for G at c^A, the class bound of (i), since r_z = renB(X, ι_Yc^A) − renG(X, c^A); the two together cover all three parts of r_z, and the second is open where (i) is open; or
      - (b) a localised lift whose moment defect and tail decay in the layer n of z rather than in the generation j, together with either the sum over z at one configuration across the domains Y, for example by assigning the sum over z at each member to a single re-summation, or a per-Y bound of the weight-change part. The decay of the lift does not touch the blocking-artifact part β^B_{j+1}b_{h_z}(ι_Yc^A), whose weight-change part is a property of h_z at the scale j, so without one of these (b) doesn't work. The decay rate e^{−δ₀M₁L^j} of the Cauchy note's §9.8 is set by the collar of X^∼, M₁-cubes at the scale j (its *The consequences.*), so a lift whose decay is in n needs collars at the scale of the layer n, a change of the lift's construction.
    - G is analytic, with Bałaban's bound of run B's term, where the lifted pair lies in run B's domain of the term. At the configurations where (i) evaluates it, this is the lift condition of the Cauchy note's §5 (*The lift condition*). It is derived at the single-scale real minimisers through the Cauchy note's §9.7, given the localised lift of its §9.8, with the current of the pair lift (the Cauchy note's *The lift condition*). The members of 𝒞 in a young history (§1), and the composites of (1.59) whose cube meets more than one layer, are multiscale minimisers, and at them it rests on the fine side at multiscale data, the first open input of (ii). It is open at complex configurations. RG I (1.17) does not give it, since it concerns one lattice.
    - Let 𝒞 := {U″_k, U^0_k, U_1, U_2, U_{k,Λ}, U_k} be the minimisers of the chain, with U_1 = U_{𝐁₁} and U_2 = U_{𝐁₂} the functions of the two determining sets of (1.52)–(1.53) (p.370 l.22–33). For c ∈ 𝒞, c^A and c^B are the two runs' values at equal shared variables (B, V″, V_k). Then (i) := G(X, c^A) − 𝐓^A(X, c^A) at each configuration c^A at which run A evaluates the term. These are the following.
      - U^0_k, through its representation (1.61)–(1.64) as a localised function of U_k, in which U_{1,2}, U_1, and U_{k,Λ} are expansion points (p.374 l.31–34).
      - The representation configurations of that expansion, U(𝐁″_k(Y_1), D(s)), U(𝐁_1(Y_2), ·), and U(𝐁_k(Y_3), ·), whose data D(s) are decoupled, at complex s(Δ) with |s(Δ)| ≤ e^{κ₁} (p.373 l.31–34, p.374 l.8–10 and l.26–29). They are not U^0_k.
      - The two composites U_j(𝐁_j(□₀), exp iQ(η𝐇″_k(g_kB))M^·(U^0_k)) and U_j(𝐁_j(□₀), M^·(U^0_k)) of the bracket of (1.59) (p.372 l.5–11), which are not in 𝒞.
      - For X ⊂ Z^c, the configurations of the later stages, at which (1.59)–(1.60) are repeated around U_{1,2}, then around U_1 by (1.57), and around U_{k,Λ} by (1.58) (p.373 l.2–6): the old terms at U_{1,2}, U_1, and U_{k,Λ}, and the composites with the data M^·(U_{1,2}), M^·(U_1), and M^·(U_{k,Λ}). Their new terms with the domains Y_0 are re-summed into V(Y) (p.372 l.27–33, p.373 l.10–11).
      - The decoupled configurations of the brackets of (1.60) (the open item below).
      - U_k, for the completions of p.377 l.22–26.
    - At real c^A, (i) is bounded by the class norm d_j, the lifted pair lying in run B's space by the lift condition. c^A is a local minimiser of its own data. So at the near domains inside its minimisation domain that satisfy the condition (N) of the Cauchy note's §5 it lies in the regular subclass 𝒮_z, and the class bound (5.1c) gives the age factor: (i) ≤ d_jE₁(α)L⁴L^{−β′(m−j)} per point of the layer m, β′ the age exponent of that section. At the near domains that fail (N), whose □₀ meets a finer layer, the bound is open with (R-Δ^c-T). The near domains that meet the boundary of the minimisation domain are a separate open case: ∂Ω″^∼_{h+1} for U_1 and U_2, ∂Y_1, ∂Y_2, and ∂Y_3 for the representation configurations U(𝐁″_k(Y_1), ·), U(𝐁_1(Y_2), ·), and U(𝐁_k(Y_3), ·), whose determining sets are localised in these domains, and ∂□₀ for the composites. Since Y_i = Y_{i−1} ∪ Y_i′, parts of ∂Y_1 can be parts of ∂Y_2, so the cases overlap. There c^A is not the local minimiser of its own data on □₀, so it is not in 𝒮_z (Open Gaps (3), the near domains at the boundaries of minimisation domains).
    - **Open: (i), r_z, and the lift–patch commutator at U_{1,2} near the cut, and (i) at the decoupled configurations.** U_{1,2} is U_1 on Ω″^∼_{h+1} and U_2 on its complement (LFR II (1.53), p.370 l.30), and in it "the arguments are separated completely across the boundary" ∂Ω″^∼_{h+1} (l.35). A term whose localisation domain lies on one side is a term at U_1 or at U_2. On a near domain that meets ∂Ω″^∼_{h+1}, U_{1,2} is regular (l.35–38) but is not the local minimiser of its own data, so it is not in 𝒮_z and (5.1c) gives no age factor there. U_{1,2} is not a member of 𝒞, and the patch (1.53) is not among the link maps of (ii), so its value at the entries is not defined by V^B(Y)[c]. If it is taken as the patch of ι_YU^A_1 and ι_YU^A_2, the decomposition leaves a piece that is neither (i) nor r_z: 𝐓^B(X, ι_X patch(U^A_1, U^A_2)) against 𝐓^B(X, patch(ι_YU^A_1, ι_YU^A_2)), since the lift does not commute with the patch. The decoupled configurations of (iii) are not minimisers either.
      - Where U_2 and the cut are read. "This configuration restricted to Z^c is equal to U_1" (p.371 l.26–27), so the terms with X ⊂ Z^c read at U_{1,2} are terms at U_1. U_2 and the cut enter through the expansions (1.51) and (1.54) of U″_k and U^0_k around U_{1,2} (p.370 l.39–42, p.371 l.25–26). In them J_{1,2} = J_1 on Ω″^∼_{h+1} (p.370 l.49–50), and (1.55) writes 𝐆″_kJ_{1,2} = 𝐆″_k(1 − θ)Q*_{𝐁₁}θ₁H*_{𝐁₁}J_1 + 𝐆″_kθJ_{1,2}, with θ = 1 on (Ω″^∼_{h+1})^c and on a boundary layer of width 2M₁ (p.371 l.2–5, l.19–24), so the current of U_2 enters only through 𝐆″_kθJ_{1,2}.
      - What closes it at U_{1,2}, in this order: entries and lifts of U_1 and U_2 with run B's boundary condition at ∂Ω″^∼_{h+1}, a lift on each side that the global ι does not give; r_z at U_{1,2}, by (a) or (b) of the r_z item; the commutator of that lift with the patch (1.53); and (5.1c) at the patched configuration on the near domains that meet ∂Ω″^∼_{h+1}. Bałaban's regularity there is an input, but the near-domain analysis of the Cauchy note's §5 uses the local minimiser property, which the regularity does not give. At the decoupled configurations (i) is a separate item: (iii) compares run B's operators and contains no difference G − 𝐓^A. What closes it there: an age bound of Δ at configurations that are not local minimisers, which the near-domain analysis of the Cauchy note's §5 does not give.
    - For 𝐑^{(j)} (i) is the carried R-difference of §8.4 at run A's configurations, and for 𝐁^{(j)} the carried boundary-term difference of (e). Its size is of the type of the Cauchy note's carried differences 𝒟_m, not C_hθ̃^n; for the 𝐁-terms it is the bound of (e).
  - (ii) The variation of the chain (open). Each run's V(Y) evaluates that run's old terms at that run's chain.
    - *The re-summation as a function of the chain.* The regions that the terms of V(Y) read, the cubes □₀ of (1.59) and the domains Y_4 of (1.61)–(1.65), lie in Y by the nesting Y = Y_4 (LFR II p.376 l.39), Y_4 ⊇ Y_1 ⊇ X (p.373 l.15–17, p.374 l.33), and X ⊇ Y_0 ⊇ □₀ for the second-stage terms (p.372 l.17, p.373 l.10–15). V(Y) depends on U_k restricted to Y and on the fields A, B, and V″ localised in Y (p.377 l.7–8), and its bounds (1.68)–(1.69) are on Ũ^c_k(Y, α̃₀, α̃₁) (p.377 l.8–19). The shared fields B and V″ are equal in the two runs.
      - *The field A (derived, unreviewed).* A is 𝐀₀ := 𝐇_{k,Λ}((1/i)log M^·(U_0(𝐔))), the analytic extension (p.367 l.46–49) of the field 𝔸₀ of LFR I (1.86)–(1.87), which represents U_0 inside Λ in the mixed axial and Landau gauge, U_0^{(AL)} = exp iη𝔸₀ (LFR I p.197 l.25–38). It is not the fixed point 𝐀₀ of RG II (1.3)–(1.4) in (Wδ-3). A, B, and V″ are the three fields on which U″_{k,Z″_{h+2}} depends (p.368 l.4–7).
      - On pp.355–377, A enters only as Q̃_·(η𝐀₀), with exp iQ̃_·(η𝐀₀) = M^·(U_0^{(AL)}) (p.368 l.5, (1.46), p.370 l.15–16, (1.54)). These are the data of U^0_k, the configuration (1.50) at B = 0 (p.370 l.14), on Λ ∩ Ω″^{∼2}_{h+1}, and with exp ig_kB those of U″_k.
      - So A is read through the entry of U^0_k, as the link image M^·(c_{U^0_k}) on these layers. At c^B it is run B's A, by the constraint of U^0_k. At ι_Yc^A it is run A's A, since Mι_YU = U on Y^∼ and the averagings compose, in the common gauge of §4(c) (*The gauge of the data.*), in which ι_Y is covariant (the Cauchy note's §9.8).
      - Its two runs' difference is the data difference of §4(c) through U_0, ≤ C′_Δ(M)(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁ per data point (*The data difference.*), which the coarse side d̄ of the fine-side item below contains. A is therefore not a separate variable, and no lift other than ι_Y enters it.
      - *The entries.* For an entry c = (c_i)_{c∈𝒞}, one configuration on Y^∼ per member, write V^B(Y)[c] for run B's re-summation with the following variables. This is well defined for U″_k, U^0_k, U_{k,Λ}, and U_k. For U_1 and U_2 it is not: they "are supported in the domains Ω″^∼_{h+1}, (Ω″^∼_{h+1})^c correspondingly" (p.370 l.23), so one configuration on Y^∼ per member fails where Y meets both sides, and ι_Y, a function of a configuration on Y^∼, cannot be applied to them there (open, Open Gaps (3)).
      - Where a term of V(Y) reads a member of 𝒞 as such, it reads the entry of that member. The counterterm of each renormalised combination reads the same entry as its term, so it has one configuration.
      - Every other configuration that the re-summation reads is a link image: the representation configurations of (1.61)–(1.63), the composites of (1.59) and of the later stages, the decoupled configurations of (1.60), and the fluctuation fields with their propagators. Each is run B's map ((1.51), (1.54), (1.57)–(1.64)) or run B's local minimisation at the entries from which it is built.
      - So the free variables are the entries. Run B's V(Y) is V^B(Y)[c^B], and at run B's chain the entries and the link images agree. At other entries they need not: run B's (1.61) at the entries ι_Yc^A gives U^B(𝐁″_k(Y_1), M^·(c^A)), which is not the entry ι_YU^{0,A}.
    - Put (ii) := V^B(Y)[c^B] − V^B(Y)[ι_Yc^A], with ι_Yc^A := (ι_Yc^A_i)_{c∈𝒞} and each current the pair-lift current Q₁^{pl}(ι_Yc^A_i)*J(c^A_i). Then V^B(Y)[ι_Yc^A] − V^A(Y)[c^A] is the re-summation of the following pieces. They are telescoped in this order.
      - For the brackets of (1.59), at the members and at the representation configurations, (iii) and (β) come first, at the fixed configurations ι_Yc^A and c^A, and replace run A's propagators, fixed points, and gauge transformations by run B's. (i), (iv), and (α) are then differences under run B's re-summation operator, with run B's propagated data.
      - For the brackets of (1.60), (i) comes first, at run A's decoupled configuration e^{iη𝐇^A(s)}c^A, where it replaces 𝐓^A by G. Then (iii) and (δ) compare G there with 𝐓^B at run B's decoupled field. The other order fails, since after (iii) 𝐓^A would be read at run B's decoupled field, which lives on T_{ε/L}.
      - The pieces are the same in both orders: 𝐓^B(X, e^{iη𝐇^B(s)}ι_Yc^A) − 𝐓^A(X, e^{iη𝐇^A(s)}c^A) is (iii) and (δ) plus (i) at the decoupled configuration.

      The pieces are the following.
      - (i) with its remainder r_z, where a term reads a member of 𝒞.
      - (iv) below, the variation group at the composites of (1.59) and of the later stages for X ⊂ Z^c.
      - (iii), the two runs' propagators at the configurations ι_Yc^A and c^A, through the transfer map of (iii).
      - (α) The variation group at the representation configurations of (1.61)–(1.63): run B's U(𝐁″_k(Y_1), ·), U(𝐁_1(Y_2), ·), and U(𝐁_k(Y_3), ·) at the data M^·(ι_Yc^A) = M^·(c^A) against run A's at the same data, with the gauge transformations u^0_k, u_1, and u_{k,Λ}, in the form of (iv), with (i) at run A's representation configurations (open, with the status of (iv) and the lift condition there).
      - (β) The run differences of the functions of the localisation expansions of (1.61)–(1.64), 𝐇″_k with σ̃₀, 𝐇_{𝐁₁}, and 𝐇_k (p.373 l.31–34, p.374 l.8–10 and l.26–29), and of the gauge transformations u^0_k, u_1, and u_{k,Λ} (p.373 l.29–30) (open). Their propagator parts are of the type of (iii); they also contain the run difference of the fixed points D and 𝐀₀, of the type of (Wδ-3), so (β) is not purely a propagator difference.
      - (δ) At the decoupled fine configurations of (1.60), the nonlinearity of the lift, ι_X(e^{iη𝐇^A(s)}c^A) against e^{iη𝐇^B(s)}ι_Yc^A (*(iii)*, open).
    - So (ii) and these pieces make up the two runs' difference of V(Y), up to (i), r_z, and the lift–patch commutator at U_{1,2} near the cut, (i) at the decoupled configurations (the open item of (i)), and the entries of U_1 and U_2 where Y meets both sides of ∂Ω″^∼_{h+1}.
    - V^B∘ι_Y, the single function of the background, alone does not give (i) + (iii): at run A's background it evaluates run B's chain at the lifted background, not at the lifts ι_Yc^A of run A's chain, and it leaves an equal-data variation of the type of (ii).
    - The variation term by term doesn't work: Bałaban's bound of an old term on its domain has no age factor, the renormalised terms enter (1.68) through the proof of Theorem 2 (p.376 l.40–41), whose sum over the generations needs the age decay, and the geometric-mean bound of the Cauchy note's (5.1v) gives only the power θ_V of the distance.
    - *The joint line.* For c ∈ {U″_k, U^0_k, U_{k,Λ}, U_k}, run B's constraints factor through M, every layer of the member's determining set being at a shared scale ≥ 1, and its constrained minimiser is unique up to gauge (VAR Theorem 1), so ι(Mc^B) = c^B up to gauge, as for U^B (*The intermediate current and the estimate.*). For U_1 and U_2, minimisers with boundary conditions at ∂Ω″^∼_{h+1} (p.370 l.22–25), the global one-step lift ι does not reproduce them; the identity needs a lift with the same boundary condition, which is not defined (open, with the entries of U_1 and U_2 above). Write ι_Yc^A = e^{i(ε/L)𝐒_c}c^B on Y, as in the Cauchy note's §5 (*The pair line.*). At c = U_k, 𝐒_c is the field of the Cauchy note's §9.7 with the localised lift ι_Y, of the size σ̂_{k,j} on the domain X of each term; in a young history U_k is multiscale, and this size rests on the fine side at the multiscale members of 𝒞 (open, below). The joint line moves all entries together with one parameter, c(t) := (e^{it(ε/L)𝐒_c}c^B)_{c∈𝒞}, t ∈ ℂ, with the currents frozen at run B's, and f(t) := V^B(Y)[c(t)]. The currents then move at the fixed entries ι_Yc^A, as in the frozen-current split of the Cauchy note's *The pair line.*, and (ii) is f(0) − f(1) plus that current part (*The current of the line*, below). For r(Y) ≤ ½, Cauchy's estimate gives |f(1) − f(0)| ≤ sup_{|t|≤1}|f′(t)| ≤ 2r(Y)sup|f| for f analytic for |t| < 1/r(Y). Here r(Y) := max_{c∈𝒞}sup_{x∈Y}σ_c(x)/ϱ(x), with σ_c(x) the bound of a_{j(x)}|𝐒_c(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇_{c^B}𝐒_c(x)|, ∇_{c^B} the covariant gradient at c^B, and ϱ(x) the room of *The room at real points.*. The moves at x depend on σ_c at the other points of Y, so the room is well posed through this supremum over Y.
    - *The bounds of V(Y) (quoted).* Run B's V(Y) satisfies on the whole space Ũ^c_k(Y, α̃₀, α̃₁) the bound (1.68), |V(Y, (𝐔, 𝐉))| ≤ αexp(−(1 + 2β)κd_{k,Z}(Y)), for Y ∖ Z^∼ ≠ ∅, and the bound (1.69), O(1)Σ_{j≤k}|Γ_j⁰ ∩ Y|, for Y a component of Z^∼ (p.377 l.8–19). Its arguments are the background and the shared fields; run B's chain and its links are functions of these.
    - *The count (derived).* Y ∩ Z^∼ is a union of components of Z^∼ containing at least one (p.377 l.5–6), so Y is determined by Y ∖ Z, and the tree of (1.67) meets every M-cube of the components of Y ∖ Z (p.376 l.28–30), the collar Z^∼ ∖ Z of each included component among them. Anchoring the tree at a collar cube gives Σ_Yαe^{−(1+2β)κd_{k,Z}(Y)} ≤ C(κ)α times the number of M-cubes of the collars of Z^∼, for κ large against the entropy of the trees. The collar is one layer of the cubes of which Z is a union (CRE p.245 l.40–43, p.250 l.26), so it has at most 3^d M-cubes per M-cube of Z, and each M-cube of Z contains a point of the component's history. (1.69) is per point of Γ_j⁰.
    - **Open: the variation of the chain.** Four inputs are missing.
      - The fine side at the multiscale members of 𝒞, U_k among them, the bound σ_c(x) ≤ C(ε₀)(a_k/ε)^{ε₀}d̄(x), with the localised lift ι_Y adding C(ε₀)ρ_k and its tail, as in σ̂_{k,j}. It gives the lift condition at the multiscale chain of (i) as well. The coarse side is derived where the profile of (b) applies: c^A = e^{iεA_c}Mc^B with a_{j(x)}|A_c(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇A_c(x)| ≤ d̄(x) := C_U(M)(ε/a_{min(j(x),n)})^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁, by the profile of (b) at each data, with *The kernel form of VAR Proposition 4.* (rate ⅛δ₀), and (c) through U_0. The profile needs the joins 𝐁₁ and 𝐁₂ ((2.14) [III]) to satisfy PRT2 (2.1)–(2.2), which is not checked. What closes it: the argument of the Cauchy note's §9.7 at the multiscale data, with the reference configuration e^{i(ε/L)ℒA_c}c^B, 𝔊_BJ(c^B) = 0 since run B's constraints factor through M, and the residual through PBG (3.42), (3.44) for run B's 𝔊 in the layer-weighted norm of (b).
      - The bound of f on the disc. At t ≠ 0 the entries c(t) are not run B's chain at any background, so (1.68) does not apply to f as it stands. Bałaban's analyticity, (1.65) and (1.68), is a statement about functions of one background (𝐔, 𝐉), and the links are identities only between configurations of a common background, so a replay of his proof does not give it. What closes it: a multi-variable form of (1.68) and (1.69), with V^B(Y)[c] analytic in the entries c, the link images being run B's maps and local minimisers at the entries, with the bound (1.68) on the product over 𝒞 of the spaces at the thresholds α̃′ that contain c(t) for |t| < 1/r(Y). Its proof has to redo the re-summation, the renormalisation of the proof of Theorem 2, and the decay factors of the expansions (1.51), (1.54), and (1.57)–(1.64) (p.376 l.24–42) in these variables. The renormalisation is the hard step, since the entries c(t) are not local minimisers of their own data.
      - The current of the line. The moves of c(t) are those of *The room at real points.*, with the currents frozen. The current part moves, at the fixed entries ι_Yc^A, each current linearly from J(c^B) to the pair-lift current Q₁^{pl}(ι_Yc^A)*J(c^A), by ΔJ_c. At the single-scale real minimisers the Cauchy note's *The current along the pair line* bounds it, given the localised lift, by sup|ΔJ| ≤ C_Jσ̂_{k,j}L^{3(j−k)} in the units of a_j, from the multiplier representation J = 𝒬*λ of both runs' currents. At the other members of 𝒞 the same derivation needs that representation of each run's current at the member, through the multipliers of its own constrained problem, and the fine side for 𝐒_c; it is open there. At complex configurations the representation itself is the complex lift of the Cauchy note's *Open (complex configurations)*.
      - The reading *Bałaban's Statements at the Reduced Thresholds*, which *The room at real points.* uses for (1.65) at the halves of the thresholds, and the conditions of LFR I (1.64)–(1.69) at the other configurations that the terms read (*The room at real points.*). What closes the second part: the proof of (1.65), which is that of (1.89) [IV] with ε_j replaced by the thresholds (p.375 l.24–27), passes through the extended functions (1.63) and (1.62) and the representation configurations U(𝐁_k(Y_3), ·) and U(𝐁_1(Y_2), ·) (p.375 l.1–13), so a check of its steps at the halved thresholds gives their conditions; for U″_k, U_1, U_2, and U_{k,Λ}, constrained minimisers at data in the same spaces, the same check is needed at their data (unchecked).
    - With these inputs, the count and *At real points the ratio is small.* give |(ii)| ≤ C_hθ̃^n per point for the domains at the layers ≥ n, under ε₀ < 1/125. The bound has no age factor and no sum over the generations, since the bound of V(Y) holds on the whole space and the 𝐄-, 𝐑-, and 𝐁-terms are all inside V(Y).
    - A domain Y that meets a layer below n is either not deep, and then d̄ on Y is set by its finest layer, which is > k_s/8 with the stopping scale k_s of (e), or deep, and then it is bounded in (e), *Deep V(Y) in a Young Polymer*.
  - (iii) The decoupled propagators (open). In V(Y) the old terms enter the brackets of (1.59)–(1.60) through the fluctuation field 𝐇″_k(g_kB), and LFR II applies to these brackets the decoupling expansion (6.9)–(6.10) [I], which is RG II (1.9)–(1.10) (p.372 l.15–19). Its terms evaluate each run's term at that run's 𝐇″_k, built from its H(s), G̃(s), and H₀(s), at |s(Δ)| = e^{κ₁} and on the t_□ circle of (1.22) (RG II (1.23)). The decoupled field is not a minimiser with data on a neighbourhood of Y: at s ∈ {0, 1} its propagators are the sums of the walks of (1.6) inside the cubes with s = 1, not inverses of restricted operators (RG II p.3 l.30–37), so the alignment of (b) does not apply to it. The comparison runs through the walks.
    - (iii) is taken at fixed configurations: run B's propagators at ι_Yc^A against run A's at c^A. The change of the configurations from c^B to ι_Yc^A is (ii), whose links are run B's maps along the line, so (ii) and (iii) do not overlap.
    - The two runs' propagators act into different spaces: H(s) and H₀(s) map scale-k data, and G̃(s) maps fine sources, to fields on the run's own fine lattice, T_ε for run A and T_{ε/L} for run B, the lattice of the run's fluctuation field. RG II p.5 l.6–7 gives the domain of H(s), functions on Y_0^{(k)}, and not the codomain. So the comparison needs a transfer map, and the two kinds of brackets need different ones.
      - In the brackets of (1.59) the fluctuation field enters only through the data exp iQ(η𝐇″_k(g_kB))M^·(U^0_k) of the composite (p.372 l.5–11), and Q(η𝐇″_k) is a coarse object. There the comparison is Q^A(η𝐇″^A_k) against Q^B(η𝐇″^B_k), on the lattice of the composites' data, which is common to both runs.
      - In the brackets of (1.60) and at the decoupled fine configurations the comparison is 𝐓^B(X, e^{iη𝐇^B(s)}ι_Yc^A) against G(X, e^{iη𝐇^A(s)}c^A) = 𝐓^B(X, ι_X(e^{iη𝐇^A(s)}c^A)), at the pair lift. It contains the nonlinearity of the lift, ι_X(e^{iη𝐇^A(s)}c^A) against e^{iη𝐇^B(s)}ι_Yc^A, which is not a propagator difference and which no factor bound supplies. It is the piece (δ) of (ii) (open).
    - Each walk term of (1.6) carries s only through the factor s(Δ₁)⋯s(Δ_m) of the cubes of σ₀ that meet its localisation domain (RG II p.3 l.30–32). The cubes and the localisation domains, and so these factors, are the same in both runs, since they are scale-k objects (RG II p.3 l.14–17 and l.27–33). So at complex s the two runs' transferred propagators differ by the sum over the walks of these factors times the run differences of the transferred walk terms. The difference is defined only after the transfer, and it is bounded per walk term, not per factor: (1.7) bounds whole walk terms, and PBG gives no bound per factor (`WILSON_IT1_WALK_FACTOR_DIFFERENCE_2026_10_28.md` §3(a)). If each intertwining defect of a walk term's constituents is at most δ_c times the bound of its constituent, the walk term's difference, telescoped over its at most N_c(|ω| + 1) terms, is at most N_c(|ω| + 1)max_cδ_c times its bound, and |ω| + 1 ≤ 2^{|ω|} is absorbed by doubling the factor O(M₁^{−1/2})^{|ω|}, which changes B₀ to B₀′ (that note's §7). The argument of (1.11) (p.5 l.6–16), under that note's condition (W-M) on M, then gives |(Q₁H^B(s) − H^A(s))X| ≤ δ_w(Y)B₀′e^{16κ₁}|X| on |s| ≤ e^{κ₁}, with δ_w(ω) := N_cmax_cδ_c the per-walk-term defect and δ_w(Y) its supremum over the walks ω in Y, and the same for G̃(s), H₀(s), and the fine form. This δ_w(ω) is that of the fine form (c) below, whose constant C_w contains N_c.
    - The solutions D and 𝐀₀ of (1.12)–(1.17) exist for arbitrary operators with the regularity and the bounds of the propagators, as functionals of these operators with the properties proved in [15] (RG II p.3 l.2–3). Analyticity in the operators is not stated there, and p.6 l.44–45 states analyticity in (𝐔, 𝐉) only, so analyticity in the operators on balls of fixed radius, uniformly in the configurations, is part of the open (iii).
