With it, on the segment between the two runs' transferred propagators, at equal background, Cauchy's estimate therefore moves 𝐇″_k by at most C_Hδ_w times its bound (1.18), with C_H a constant of B₀′, C₂, C₄, and κ₁.
    - On Bałaban's t_□ circle the argument (tζ̃_□ + t_□ζ_□)𝐇″_k of the old term can reach the threshold α₂ of RG I (3.31): under 4B₀C₁e^{16κ₁}ε₁ ≤ ¼α₂ the tζ̃_□ part satisfies (3.31) with ½α₂, and the t_□ζ_□ part has the norm ½α₂ on his circle (RG II p.7 l.8–11). So the circle is taken at half his radius, |t_□|4B₀C₁e^{16κ₁}g_k|B| = ¼α₂, which the radius-independence of the derivative at t_□ = 0 allows, at the cost of the factor 2 in 1/|t_□| in (1.24). There the argument is within ¾α₂, the room is at least ¼α₂, and the argument moves by at most C_Hδ_w(½α₂ + ¼α₂) = C_Hδ_w·¾α₂.
    - Cauchy's estimate along the segment, as in (ii), then bounds this part of V^B(Y) − V^A(Y) by 6C_Hδ_w times the term's bound on the smaller circle, which is at most twice Bałaban's. So it is at most 12C_Hδ_w times Bałaban's bound of the term, and, after the re-summation, at most 12C_Hδ_w times the bound (1.68) of V(Y).
    - The missing inequality is (W-δ), in the per-walk-term fine form (c) of `WILSON_IT1_WALK_FACTOR_DIFFERENCE_2026_10_28.md` (with the averaged form (b) through χ_AQ₁, and for the brackets of (1.59) the coarse images): for each walk ω in Y and each of H, G̃, and H₀, run A's walk term at the chain configurations c^A and run B's at their lift ι_Yc^A differ, through the transfer ℒ_ι = dι_Y(Ũ), by at most δ_w(ω) times the bound of the walk term. Here δ_w(ω) ≤ C_w(ε/a_{m(ω)})^{γ_w}, γ_w := min(β, (1 − ε₀)/3), with m(ω) the finest global layer that ∪X̃_i⁵ meets, β < 1 the Hölder exponent of (Wδ-1), and C_w that note's constant of M, β, L, d, and Bałaban's constants (its §8); γ_w > 1/4 if β > 1/4 and ε₀ < 1/4. Run B's walk terms are taken at ι_Yc^A, the lift of run A's background. Their move from c^B, the fine side of size σ_c, is part of (ii), whose links are run B's maps along the line, and is carried through r(Y). So only the lattice part of that note's §5 enters (its §5 l.124), and there γ_w = β; the minimum stays a valid bound. For the brackets of (1.60), (W-δ) leaves the piece (δ) of (ii). With (W-δ), (iii) is at most 12C_HC_w(ε/a_{m(Y)})^{γ_w} times the bound (1.68), which has the form of (ii) and gives C_hθ̃^n per point for the domains at the layers ≥ n, as in *At real points the ratio is small.*. Bałaban does not supply (W-δ): (1.7) bounds one run's factors, and the two runs' factors are built from operators with k and k + 1 averaging steps from different fine lattices, which his papers do not compare. What closes it: the walk-factor note states (W-δ) per walk term in the averaged and the fine form and reduces it, through the exact intertwined identities of its §4(a)–(b), to the three open inequalities (Wδ-1)–(Wδ-3) (Open Gaps item (7)). The resolvent identity R^B − R^A = −R^B(δ𝒪)R^A with a kernel bound of δ𝒪 doesn't work: the two runs' operators act on different lattices, and no common-space difference of them has a small kernel.
  - (iv) The variation group at the composites (open with the inputs of the Cauchy note's §5 and the lift condition at the composites).
    - At the entries ι_Yc^A run B computes the composites of (1.59) by its own local minimisation at the data D := M^·(ι_Yc^A) = M^·(c^A) on □₀ ⊂ Y for the second composite and exp iQ(η𝐇″_k(g_kB))D for the first, at equal fluctuation field, the two runs' fluctuation fields differing by (iii). The same holds for the composites of the later stages with the data M^·(U_{1,2}), M^·(U_1), and M^·(U_{k,Λ}) for X ⊂ Z^c (p.373 l.2–6). So V^B(Y)[ι_Yc^A] evaluates the renormalised 𝐓^B at U^B_{j+1}(□₀, D), where V^A(Y)[c^A] evaluates the renormalised 𝐓^A at U^A_j(□₀, D).
    - Put (iv) := renB(X, U^B_{j+1}(□₀, D)) − renG(X, U^A_j(□₀, D)), the renormalised terms of run B and of G. The two runs' terms at the composites then differ by (iv) plus (i) at U^A_j(□₀, D). (iv) is the Cauchy note's variation group at equal data (§5, (5.1v)), irr^B − irr^G with the two runs' local minimisers on □₀ in place of (U^A_k, U^B_{k+1}) and the minimiser distance of §9.7 for the local problem of □₀, as in §2(c). It contains r_z at the composites only as the identity (iv) = [renB(X, U^B_{j+1}(□₀, D)) − renB(X, ι_YU^A_j(□₀, D))] + r_z(U^A_j(□₀, D)); no standalone r_z arises, and the bracket alone has no age bound at its second value. The composites have the spaces of RG I's Lemma 4, since LFR II applies "all the transformations and estimates of Sects. 3–5 [I]" to the bracket of (1.59) (p.372 l.12–13), and these are the spaces of the Cauchy note's §5.
    - So (iv) is bounded as there: at z ∈ Ω_n ∖ Ω_{n+1}, at the age (L^{j−n})^{4+β′/2} per scale-j point, with r_{0,n} in place of r_{0,k}, the single scale k of the Cauchy note being replaced by the layer n of z. Its status is that of the Cauchy note's §5: derived given the tail of the localised lift at the multiscale chain (§9.8 there), the lift condition at the composites, (R-Δ^c-T), the age factor of (5.1c) at the near domains that meet ∂□₀ (Open Gaps (3)), and, where □₀ meets more than one layer, the fine side at multiscale data of (ii). The lift condition at the composites is open: the Cauchy note lists the scale-j version of its §9.7 for the composites, ι(U^A_j(□₀, ·)) against U^B_{j+1}(□₀, ·), as open, not only at complex configurations, and the internal composites of RG I's Lemma 4 are at complex arguments anyway. The piece (α) of (ii) is the same group at the representation configurations of (1.61)–(1.63), with the same status and the lift condition there open.
    - *The rate of (iv) and (α) in the layer (derived given the inputs of (iv)).* In this bullet n is the birth scale of the history, m ≥ n the layer of z (called n in the bullet above), and j the generation of a term, as in (5.1v), not the index of the composites above. Counting the L^{4(m−j)} scale-j points per scale-m point, as in the Cauchy note's §5 after (5.1v) but without the stopping-lattice factor N_s⁴, (5.1v) at the layer m gives per point |(iv)| ≤ E_VΣ_{j≤m}[(σ̂_{m,j}/r_{0,m})^{θ_V} + (C_Π(j)ε₁²e^{−δ₀M₁L^j})^{θ_V}]L^{−(β′/2)(m−j)}, with θ_V = β′/(2(4 + β′)), about 0.053 at β′ = 19/40.
      - The configuration part. σ̂_{m,j} is σ_c of the fine side (Open Gaps (3), first sub-bullet) plus the localised lift's C(ε₀)ρ_k and its tail (second sub-bullet), and r_{0,m} ≥ ε_m. As in *At real points the ratio is small.*, with ε₁ = C(M)R_kε_k of (c), ε_k ≤ 2^{p₀}(1 + β₀)(k − m + 1)^{1/2}ε_m by CRE (2.6), and k < 8n + j(C), σ_c/ε_m ≤ C(ε₀)C_U(M)C(M)2^{p₀}(1 + β₀)R_k(k − m + 1)^{1/2}L^{j(C)ε₀}L^{−(1−25ε₀)n/3}; where §1's ε₁ is the larger, the polynomial factor of that bullet replaces R_k. The open sub-bullets must give ρ_k at the same size, and the tail at most at that size plus C_tε₁e^{−cδ₀M₁L^j}, c > 0, the form of the single-scale tail in σ̂_{k,j}; the second part is the tail part below. Since θ̃ ≥ L^{−θ_V/8} (the Cauchy note's §8.7), L^{−θ_V(1−25ε₀)n/3} ≤ θ̃^nL^{−5θ_V(1−40ε₀)n/24}, with 1 − 40ε₀ > 0 under ε₀ < 1/125. The slack absorbs the factors R_k^{θ_V} and (k − m + 1)^{θ_V/2} ≤ ((1 + j(C))8n)^{θ_V/2}, and the sum over j gives (1 − L^{−β′/2})^{−1}.
      - The moment part. C_Π(j)ε₁² ≤ max(1, k − j) ≤ 8n + j(C) (the Cauchy note's §5, at the ε₁ of (T-Λ) with q₀ > p₀ + r, *The lift condition.* there). The generations j ≤ m/2 give ≤ (8n + j(C))^{θ_V}L^{−β′m/4}(1 − L^{−β′/2})^{−1}. The generations j > m/2 give ≤ (8n + j(C))^{θ_V}e^{−θ_Vδ₀M₁L^{m/2}}(1 − e^{−θ_Vδ₀M₁})^{−1}, since L^j − L^{m/2} ≥ j − m/2 for L ≥ e. With m ≥ n, L^{−β′m/4} ≤ θ̃^nL^{−(2β′−θ_V)n/8}, and e^{−θ_Vδ₀M₁L^{n/2}}θ̃^{−n} is bounded in n. The slack absorbs the polynomial factor. Where §1's ε₁ is the larger, ε₁² gains the square of the polynomial factor of *At real points the ratio is small.*, which the slack absorbs as well.
      - The tail part. The part C_tε₁e^{−cδ₀M₁L^j} of σ̂_{m,j} contributes, by (x + y)^{θ_V} ≤ x^{θ_V} + y^{θ_V} and r_{0,m} ≥ ε_m, at most (C_tε₁/ε_m)^{θ_V}Σ_{j≤m}e^{−cθ_Vδ₀M₁L^j}L^{−(β′/2)(m−j)} ≤ (C_tε₁/ε_m)^{θ_V}[L^{−β′m/4}(1 − L^{−β′/2})^{−1} + e^{−cθ_Vδ₀M₁L^{m/2}}(1 − e^{−cθ_Vδ₀M₁})^{−1}], by the split of the moment part. Here ε₁/ε_m ≤ C(M)R_k2^{p₀}(1 + β₀)(k − m + 1)^{1/2} by ε₁ = C(M)R_kε_k and the ratio ε_k/ε_m of the configuration part, or the polynomial factor of that part where §1's ε₁ is the larger, and the slack of the moment part absorbs its power θ_V.
      - The result. |(iv)| ≤ C_{iv}θ̃^n per point at the layers ≥ n. C_{iv} depends on M, ḡ, β₀, p₀, L, r, β′, ε₀, δ₀M₁, E_V, and Bałaban's constants. It depends on j(C) through L^{θ_Vε₀j(C)} times a polynomial in j(C) and in log(ḡ^{−2} + j(C)): the first factor comes from (a_k/ε)^{ε₀} ≤ L^{j(C)ε₀}L^{8nε₀} in σ_c, which the slack in n cannot remove, and the second from log g_k^{−2} ≤ log(ḡ^{−2} + b′(K − k) + C) (ONE_LOOP_PROOFS Lemma 2.1(i)) and K − k < 8n + j(C). It does not depend on k, K, ε, or the volume, and the callers use it at fixed j(C). This is the form h_n = C_hθ̃^n of (LF-2), per point of the history at every scale (the Cauchy note's §8.6), with C_h enlarged by C_{iv}. The history's finest layer, at the scale n − 1 (the Cauchy note's §8.6), gets the same bound with C_{iv} multiplied by L^{β′/4}: there d̄ carries the factor L^{(1−ε₀)/3} more, L^{−β′m/4} the factor L^{β′/4} more, and θ_V(1 − ε₀)/3 ≤ β′/4. A domain at the layers > k_s/8 that is not deep gets ≤ C_{iv}θ̃^{k_s/8} per point in the same way. (α) is the same group at the representation configurations of (1.61)–(1.63) and has the same bound. Both still rest on the open inputs of (iv): the tail of the localised lift at the multiscale chain, the fine side, the lift condition at the composites and at the representation configurations, (R-Δ^c-T), and the age factor of (5.1c) at the near domains that meet ∂□₀ and the boundaries ∂Y_i of the representation configurations (Open Gaps (3)).
  - The room at real points (derived at U^0_k given the reading *Bałaban's Statements at the Reduced Thresholds*, except the current). Bałaban's C₀ and C₁ of (2.28) are "sufficiently large" (p.259 l.50), that is, bounded below only, so they are taken at least λ_Q := 16B₃²C_{Q*} times his lower bounds, C_{Q*} the constant of the Cauchy note's pair lift (§5, *Complex domain*). Then his statements hold at the thresholds α̃′ := α̃/(4B₃) of the Cauchy note's common polydisc and at their halves α̃′/2, the radii r of its norm class, as well (*The room at complex points.*, *Bałaban's Statements at the Reduced Thresholds*). In particular (1.65) holds with α̃₀′/2 and α̃₁′/2 in both of its spaces, its proof being that of (1.89) [IV] with ε_j replaced by the thresholds (p.375 l.25–27). The integers q₀ and q₁ of (2.28) are bounded below only ("greater than 1", p.259 l.48–50), so q₀, q₁ > p₀ is a choice that (2.28) allows, and it is made here. The real backgrounds of the support lie in Ũ^c_k(Y₄, α̃₀′/2, α̃₁′/2), as they lie in the spaces (1.64)–(1.69) of LFR I (§3(d), the item on (1.64)–(1.69)), since α′_{i,m}/(2ε_m) = (C_i/(8B₃A₀))(log g_m^{−2})^{q_i−p₀} also exceeds every constant for γ small (CRE (2.4), (2.28)). So at a real point U^0_k, with its current, satisfies the conditions of LFR I (1.64)–(1.69) for n = N (p.375 l.24–25) with the halves α̃′/2 of the thresholds, by (1.65), which is a statement about U^0_k (p.375 l.17–21). For the other configurations at which the terms are read, the other members of 𝒞 and the representation configurations of (1.61)–(1.63), Bałaban states only that the extended functions (1.63) and (1.62) have regularity "almost the same" as U(𝐁_k(Y_3), M^·(𝐔)) and U(𝐁_1(Y_2), M^·(𝐔)) (p.375 l.1–13), and their conditions are the fourth open input of (ii). The composites are treated in (iv). Each conditioned quantity is below ½ϖ times its threshold, where ϖ ≥ 1 − 4β is the prefactor of LFR I (1.64)–(1.68) at the point (pp.190–191). β := 1/8 is taken, in CRE's class (2.34)–(2.39) and in LFR I's tests alike, which Bałaban's β ≤ 1/4 (p.191 l.35) allows, so ϖ ≥ ½ and the room in each condition is at least ¼ of its threshold. CRE p.261 l.42 asks only that β be "a small positive constant, but not too small, e.g., we can take β = 1/4"; *The choice β = 1/8, checked.* below checks that no step needs more. The old terms are analytic on Ũ″^c_k(Y₁, α̃₀, α̃₁) with Bałaban's bounds (p.375 l.29–35), so for the joint line of (ii), which starts at run B's chain c^B, it suffices that each of its configurations stays in the space at the thresholds α̃′. At a point x of the layer m the thresholds of that space carry α′_{i,j} with j ≥ m and factors ≥ 1. Here the layer of x is the index of the scale factors of its region. In (1.66) the region (Ω_l ∖ Ω_{l+1}) ∩ X, l = k₀ + 1, …, j, carries the factors (L^jη)^{−p} of the scale j with L_0^{2(j−l)} (p.190 l.38–48), and after the j − h preliminary integrations the determining set 𝔹_k^{(j−h)} of LFR I (1.16) (p.180 l.19–26) contains (Ω_{j+1}^c ∖ Z_j″)^{(j)}, whose variables are at the scale j. So the data of the configuration there is at the scale j, and its layer there is j. The cascade test (1.24) with the thresholds ε_j there (pp.181–182) is a test on these variables and does not by itself fix the layer. (1.66) imposes the same scale-j thresholds on the determining-set configurations U_{p,X}(M^·(𝕌)) = U(𝐁_p(X) ∪ {Γ_i^{(n)}}_{i<p}, M^·(𝕌)) and their currents there (p.190 l.18, l.38–48), so the layer that governs d̄ and σ_c there is j as well. In the units of a layer l < j these thresholds would be smaller by (L_0²/L^p)^{j−l} < 1, since L_0² ≤ L/3 (LFR I p.191 l.35). And α′_{i,j} ≥ α′_{i,m}/(1 + β₀), since g_m ≤ (1 + β₀)g_j and g(log g^{−2})^{q_i} increases for g small. With α_m := min(α′_{0,m}, α′_{1,m}), every threshold at x is therefore at least α_m/(1 + β₀) times its scale factor. Along the joint line, at |t|σ_c(x) ≤ c_ρα_m, inside the analyticity domains of the maps below, the moves of each configuration c(t) are as follows.
    - In the decomposition c(t) = U′U with U := c^B and A′ := t𝐒_c on the lattice T_{ε/L}, the G-valued part and the cube-gauge regularity condition (1.69) are unchanged, |A′| ≤ |t|σ_c(x)/a_m, and |∇_UA′| ≤ |t|σ_c(x)/a_m².
    - The plaquettes of c(t) move by ≤ 4|t|σ_c(x)(ε/(La_m))². Run B's U_{p,X}(M^·(c(t))) and J_{p,X}(M^·(c(t))) are the configuration and current of the determining set 𝐁_p(X) ∪ {Γ_i}_{i<p} at the data M^·(c(t)) (p.190 l.17–18), so they move with the data: U_{p,X} by (3.133) with the count of (b), and J_{p,X} = 𝐐*λ through its multipliers by Cauchy's estimate, as in *The multipliers.* So each of these quantities moves by ≤ C_p|t|σ_c(x) times its scale factor, with C_p ≥ 4 a constant of B₀, c_K, and the constants c̄_e, c_W, C_r, and C_ℒ′ of *The multipliers.*
    - The currents are frozen along the configuration line of (ii) and move only in its current part, by ΔJ_c, which is the open current of (ii) away from the single-scale real minimisers.

    Each move other than the current is therefore ≤ C_p|t|σ_c(x) times its scale factor. So, the currents being moved separately, ϱ(x) ≥ c_ρα_m at the real points x of the layer m, with c_ρ := 1/(4(1 + β₀)C_p). Then each move is at most ¼ of its threshold, and the joint line stays in the space at the thresholds α̃′ for |t| ≤ 1/r(Y).
  - *The choice β = 1/8, checked.* Every bare β of the transcriptions of CRE, LFR I, and LFR II was read (119 occurrences on 28 pages: 29 on 10 pages of CRE, 59 on 11 of LFR I, and 31 on 7 of LFR II).
    - The upper bounds hold at 1/8: LFR I p.182 l.17 (0 < β ≤ ½), p.191 l.34–35 and p.198 l.16 (β ≤ 1/4), p.199 l.13 (¾ < 1 − β), p.200 l.22–23 (21/8 ≤ 3(1 − β/2) and ≤ 4(1 − β/2)), p.200 l.28 and l.39 (room 1 − β), p.187 l.25–31 (β ≤ ½), and LFR II p.376 l.5–6 ((1 − β)L ≥ 1 + 3β at L ≥ 2).
    - RG I lies outside this count. Its class parameter, written β_I as in the Cauchy note, enters the margins of its Lemma 4 (pp.275–280), and the Cauchy note's §5 takes β_I ≤ 1/4. Its upper bounds hold there at L ≥ 2: (1 + 7β_I)L^{−2} ≤ 1 (p.279 l.28, "for β not too large") and (1 + 8β_I)L^{−2} ≤ 1 (p.280 l.18), which are 11/16 and ¾ at β_I = 1/4 and L = 2. Its other restrictions, O(1)Mα₁ ≤ β_I (p.278 l.46) and (4B₃²O(1)M)²α₀ ≤ β_I (p.278 l.64), are choices of α₀ and α₁ made after β_I.
    - The steps that need β not too small need only β > 0 with another constant chosen after it: κ large at LFR II p.389 l.12 (βκ ≥ 6·2^d), and LFR II p.389 l.39 and p.388 l.34; R large at LFR I p.184 l.4 (B₃e^{−2δM₂R_j}22d² < β/10); the gains (1 + cβ)κ of LFR I p.192 l.11, CRE p.262 l.27–28, and LFR II pp.376–391; and the margin α₃ of RG I p.280, proportional to β, through the thresholds of kind γ small. These choices are made after β.
    - The steps homogeneous in β hold for every β: LFR I (1.31) (p.184 l.8–9, at L² > 2), and p.186 l.42–p.187 l.13, where the small factors are chosen as αβ (p.186 l.42) and the step needs 8α ≤ 1 (p.187 l.13).
    - CRE p.276 l.30 enlarges the class by 1 + β, and the definitions of CRE p.261 l.15–37 and LFR I pp.181–182 and pp.186–191 carry β as a parameter.
    - The other letters β of these pages are other constants: the exponent of Theorem 2 (CRE p.263 l.16–42, p.281 l.45, p.283 l.28–32, p.284 l.15), the β-function (CRE p.255 l.52, p.282 l.43), the coupling parameter of CRE p.246 l.17–18, β′ of CRE (2.6)–(2.9), and the exponent of LFR II p.357 l.10, where any β > 0 holds.
  - The room at complex points (open). The class norms of the Cauchy note's §8.2 and §8.6 are taken on its norm classes at the radii r = α′/2 = α/(8B₃): d_j is the smallest number with the bound of §8.2 on U^c_j(X, r_{0,j}, r_{1,j}), and the same holds for the norms of the 𝐑- and 𝐁-terms and for (LF-2). A bound derived on a class holds on its norm class, and the real backgrounds of the support lie in the norm classes (*The room at real points.*), so every use at real configurations is unchanged. The sub-bullets derive the coarse alignment at the points of the norm class and reduce the joint line of (ii) there to the complex lift. The class norms are evaluated only at run A's configurations, in (i). *The Room* puts U^0_k and U_k in run A's half of the norm class; the lift half is the open lift condition, and the composites and the decoupled configurations are not covered by it. What is missing: (1) the open inputs of (ii) at complex points, *The Fine Side at Complex Points*; (2) the inequality (W-δ) of (iii), for the split of each V(Y); (3) *Bałaban's Statements at the Reduced Thresholds* is a reading of the pages it cites, not a check of every use of C₀ and C₁ in his proofs. The class bound (5.1c) at run A's configurations is derived in the Cauchy note's §5 at the near domains satisfying (N), unreviewed, with (R-Δ^c-T) open at the others.
    - *Bałaban's Statements at the Reduced Thresholds.* The thresholds α_{0,j} and α_{1,j} enter Bałaban's construction only through its analyticity spaces. In CRE these are (2.28), the spaces of p.261, and (3.43) (p.259 l.40–50, p.261, p.276 l.24–31). In LFR I they are the spaces Ũ^{(n)c}_k (p.195 l.22–24), and in LFR II the extensions of p.358 l.27–29 and p.366 l.25–28 and the spaces of (1.65) (p.375 l.17–35). The cutoffs are set by ε_k and ϑ_k (CRE (3.16); LFR I p.193 l.21–23). So the effective actions do not depend on C₀ and C₁, and by the uniqueness of analytic continuation the extensions at smaller C₀ and C₁ are the restrictions of those at larger ones. Bałaban's conditions on C₀ and C₁ are lower bounds (p.259 l.50), with γ small depending on them. CRE p.262 l.43–46 refers the restrictions on α_{0,j} and α_{1,j} to RG I, where they are upper bounds on α, such as O(1)Mα₁ ≤ β_I and (4B₃²O(1)M)²α₀ ≤ β_I, with β_I RG I's class parameter, met for γ small and weaker at smaller thresholds. His conditions B₃α₃ < ½α₁ and 2B₃α₃ ≤ β_IL^{−2}α₀ on α₃ (RG I p.280) are homogeneous. The same sentence of CRE continues "and we will introduce some new restrictions also". The restrictions read here are of three kinds: upper bounds on the thresholds, weaker at smaller ones; lower bounds against ε_j and ϑ_j, as in *The Margin.* below, met for γ small at α_{i,j}/μ since α_{i,j}/ε_j grows as a power of log g_j^{−2}; and homogeneous relations among the thresholds. The new restrictions of CRE, LFR I, and LFR II are not each checked against these kinds. So with C₀ and C₁ at least λ_Q = 16B₃²C_{Q*} times his lower bounds, as *The room at real points.* takes them, his statements hold at the thresholds α_{i,j}/μ for the five values μ ∈ {1, 4B₃, 8B₃, 16B₃², 8B₃C_{Q*}} that the notes use (λ_Q ≥ μ for each, with B₃, a constant of upper bounds, taken ≥ ½, and C_{Q*} ≥ 1), with α₂ and α₃ divided by μ with them, and his bounds at his thresholds restrict to these smaller classes. This covers the common polydisc α′_{i,j} = α_{i,j}/(4B₃) of the Cauchy note's §5, its norm class r_{i,j} = α_{i,j}/(8B₃), the current bound r_{0,j}/C_{Q*} of the norm class, and the radii α_{i,j}/(16B₃²) of its class (b). In particular the inclusions that the Cauchy note's norms use hold there: RG I Lemma 4 (p.280, (3.53)) with α₃/μ, CRE (3.43), the class at the next scale (p.262 l.26–30, p.279 l.22–23), and (1.65) with α̃₀′/2 and α̃₁′/2 in both of its spaces.
    - *The Margin.* The Cauchy note's §8.2 runs R-Δ^c at the radii r of the norm class and takes the margin m_k := α_{3,k}/2 of that run, and places the cutoff ball of (3.16) inside it with the ratio T₀ := m_k/ϑ_k ≥ 2. By ϑ_k = (A₁/A₀)ε_k, ε_k = g_kA₀(log g_k^{−2})^{p₀} (CRE (2.4)), and α_{3,k} ≥ c_mg_k(log g_k^{−2})^{min(q₀,q₁)}, α_{3,k}/ϑ_k ≥ (c_m/A₁)(log g_k^{−2})^{min(q₀,q₁)−p₀}. Since q₀, q₁ > p₀ by the choice of *The room at real points.*, α_{3,k}/ϑ_k ≥ 4 for γ small, so T₀ ≥ 2.
    - *The Room.* At a point (𝐔, 𝐉) of Ũ^c_k(Y₄, α̃₀′/2, α̃₁′/2), (1.65) at these thresholds puts U^0_k, with its current, in Ũ″^c_k(Y₁, α̃₀′/2, α̃₁′/2); the other members of 𝒞 are as at real points (the fourth open input of (ii)). The old terms are analytic on Ũ″^c_k(Y₁, α̃₀, α̃₁) with Bałaban's bounds (p.375 l.29–35). So each conditioned quantity of LFR I (1.64)–(1.68) is below ½ϖ times its threshold, and the room in each condition is at least ¼ of its threshold, as at real points. For U_k itself, the point (𝐔, 𝐉), this holds directly, since Ũ^c_k(Y₄, α̃₀′/2, α̃₁′/2) imposes the conditions at the points of Y.
    - *The Alignment Field at Complex Points.* Let z ↦ (𝐔(z), 𝐉(z)) be the line of *The complex class.* through (𝐔, 𝐉), and A(z) the alignment field of run A's configuration of the chain at the point z of the line against Ũ(z). For a point x and a complex-linear functional ℓ of norm 1, f(z) := ℓ(a_{j(x)}A(z)(x)) is analytic for |z| < 25 (*The disc.*). Let d̄′(x) be the coarse-side bound d̄(x) of (ii) with ε₁′ for ε₁. The real points x with |x| < 25 are regular with 2ε₁′ for ε₁, by the count of *The real points.* with 26 for 25 and (T-c), so |f(x)| ≤ 2d̄′(x) ≤ 2C_U(M)ε₁′ there. The line's data at z and at Re z differ only through i Im(z)A′_I, by ≤ 2C_c(α̂₀ + α̂₁) ≤ 2ε₁′ times their scale factors, since C_c(α̂₀ + α̂₁) ≤ ε′_k ≤ ε₁′. So each run's configuration of the chain at z differs from that at Re z by ≤ 2C_pε₁′, as U_{p,X} moves with its data in *The room at real points.* Writing U^A(z)Ũ(z)^{−1} = [U^A(z)U^A(Re z)^{−1}][U^A(Re z)Ũ(Re z)^{−1}][Ũ(Re z)Ũ(z)^{−1}] gives |f| ≤ M_c := (2C_U(M) + 5C_p)ε₁′ on the disc, the fifth C_pε₁′ covering the commutators of the three factors. M_c ≤ 1 for γ small. The Lemma (Two Constants), with 𝔪 = 2d̄′(x) and 𝔐 = 1, gives |f(i)| ≤ (2d̄′(x))^{15/16} ≤ 2d̄′(x)^{15/16}, and the same holds for a_{j(x)}²∇A. So at (𝐔, 𝐉), a_{j(x)}|A(x)| + a_{j(x)}²|∇A(x)| ≤ 𝔡(x) := 4d̄′(x)^{15/16}. This is the coarse input of the fine side at complex points.
    - *The Fine Side at Complex Points (open).* The joint line of (ii) at the point z of the line needs ι_Yc^A(z) = e^{i(ε/L)𝐒_c(z)}c^B(z) with a bound on 𝐒_c(z). Where both sides are analytic on the disc, 𝐒_c(z) is analytic there, and the Lemma (Two Constants) applies to it as to A(z) above, with 𝔪 = 2σ_c′(x), σ_c′(x) the bound σ_c(x) of (ii) with ε₁′ for ε₁, since the real points of the disc are regular with 2ε₁′, and 𝔐 = 1, giving (2σ_c′(x))^{15/16} ≤ 2σ_c′(x)^{15/16} for a_{j(x)}|𝐒_c(x)| and for a_{j(x)}²|∇𝐒_c(x)|, and 𝔰(x) := 8σ_c′(x)^{15/16}, the sum of the two bounds doubled. The moves of *The room at real points.* then hold at (𝐔, 𝐉) with 𝔰(x) for σ_c(x), the factor 2 covering the commutator of e^{it(ε/L)𝐒_c} with e^{iξA′}, and the plaquettes, U_{p,X}, and J_{p,X} move through (3.133) and the multipliers at complex arguments (VAR Prop. 6). What is missing: the lifts ι_Yc^A(z) at the complex points with their analyticity on the disc, which is the complex lift of the Cauchy note's *Open (complex configurations)*, and the open inputs of (ii) at the real points of the line.
    - *The Ratio.* 𝔰(x)/α_m ≤ 8C(ε₀)^{15/16}C_U(M)^{15/16}(a_k/ε)^{15ε₀/16}(ε/a_{min(m,n)})^{5(1−ε₀)/16}(ε₁′/α_m)ε₁′^{−1/16}. As in *At real points the ratio is small.*, ε₁′/α_m is at most C(M)(k − m + 1)^{1/2} times a power of log g_k^{−2}, by (2.4)–(2.6) and (2.28). Since ε₁′ ≥ ε_k ≥ g_k and g_k^{−2} ≤ ḡ^{−2} + b′(K − k) + C (ONE_LOOP_PROOFS Lemma 2.1(i)), ε₁′^{−1/16} ≤ (ḡ^{−2} + b′(K − k) + C)^{1/32}. With k < 8n + j(C), which is k < 8n up to the stopping scale and k ≤ K = k_s + j(C) < 8n + j(C) beyond it, and K − k < 8n + j(C), (a_k/ε)^{15ε₀/16} = L^{15kε₀/16} ≤ L^{15j(C)ε₀/16}L^{15nε₀/2}, so the power of L^{−n} is 5(1 − ε₀)/16 − 15ε₀/2 = 5(1 − 25ε₀)/16. The other factors are polynomial in k obeying k^p ≤ (1 + j(C))^p(8n)^p and are absorbed by L^{−(1−121ε₀)n/32}, and L^{−5(1−25ε₀)n/16} ≤ θ̃^nL^{−(1−121ε₀)n/16} since θ̃ ≥ L^{−(1−ε₀)/4}, with 1 − 121ε₀ > 0 under ε₀ < 1/125. So r(Y) ≤ ½ for n ≥ n₀′, with n₀′ depending on M, ḡ, β₀, p₀, c_ρ, L, r, ε₀, j(C), and Bałaban's constants, and with the missing inputs the variation of (ii), 2r(Y) times the bound of V(Y), is ≤ C_hθ̃^n per point at the complex points of the norm class, with C_h enlarged by a constant of these. The domains that meet a layer m < n are treated as at real points, with ≤ C_hθ̃^{k_s/8} per point if they are not deep.
  - At real points the ratio is small. α_m ≥ ε_m, since q₀, q₁ > p₀ and C₀, C₁ ≥ 4B₃A₀ (CRE (2.4), (2.28)), choices that (2.28) allows because it bounds q₀, q₁, C₀, and C₁ below only (p.259 l.48–50). By (2.6) (p.255 l.42–50), g_k ≤ (1 + β₀)(k − m + 1)^{1/2}g_m and g_m ≤ (1 + β₀)g_k, so log g_m^{−2} ≥ ½log g_k^{−2} for g small and ε_k ≤ 2^{p₀}(1 + β₀)(k − m + 1)^{1/2}ε_m. Hence, with σ_c ≤ C(ε₀)(a_k/ε)^{ε₀}d̄ of (ii), σ_c(x)/ϱ(x) ≤ c_ρ^{−1}C(ε₀)(a_k/ε)^{ε₀}C_U(M)C(M)2^{p₀}(1 + β₀)R_k(k − m + 1)^{1/2}(ε/a_{min(m,n)})^{(1−ε₀)/3}. This takes ε₁ = C(M)R_kε_k of (c). Where the ε₁ of §1 is the larger, ε₁/ε_k is at most C(1 + β₀)(k_s − k + 1)^{1/2}(1 + A₁/A₀) instead, as in the result for W_ext, polynomial in n since k_s − k < 7n, and it is absorbed with the other polynomial factors below. With k < 8n + j(C) (*The Ratio.* above), (a_k/ε)^{ε₀} = L^{kε₀} ≤ L^{j(C)ε₀}L^{8nε₀}, so the power of L^{−n} is (1 − ε₀)/3 − 8ε₀ = (1 − 25ε₀)/3. R_k ≤ L(log g_k^{−2})^r (CRE (2.5)) and the factor (k − m + 1)^{1/2} ≤ (1 + j(C))^{1/2}(8n)^{1/2} are absorbed by L^{−(1−97ε₀)n/24}. For the domains at the layers ≥ n the variation of (ii), 2r(Y) times the bound of V(Y), is therefore ≤ C_hθ̃^n per point, since L^{−(1−25ε₀)n/3} ≤ θ̃^nL^{−(1−97ε₀)n/12} for θ̃ ≥ L^{−(1−ε₀)/4}, with 1 − 97ε₀ > 0 under ε₀ < 1/125, once r(Y) ≤ ½, which holds for n ≥ n₀, with n₀ depending on M, ḡ, β₀, p₀, c_ρ, L, r, ε₀, j(C), and Bałaban's constants; j(C) enters since log g_k^{−2} ≤ log(ḡ^{−2} + b′(K − k) + C) (ONE_LOOP_PROOFS Lemma 2.1(i)) and K − k < 8n + j(C), and no dependence on k, K, ε, or the volume arises. For n < n₀, each run's variation is ≤ Bałaban's bound, and the factor θ̃^{−n₀} is absorbed in C_h. A domain that meets a layer m < n has the variation ≤ C_hθ̃^m ≤ C_hθ̃^{k_s/8} per point if it is not deep, and is bounded in (e), *Deep V(Y) in a Young Polymer*, if it is. At real points the age bound of (i) at run A's configurations is Bałaban's at the regular configurations. The bound rests on the open inputs of (ii), and, for the per-Y split, on (iii), which is open. At the complex points of the class the variation rests also on the room at complex points.
  - The boundary terms generated at this step (p.372 l.31–33) are such re-summed terms, so (i)–(ii) apply to them at their generation.
- *The complex class.* (LF-2) needs the comparisons of (b)–(d) at the complex configurations of the inductive class, since the Cauchy note's §8.6 bounds class norms. For the minimisers, their lifts, the actions, and the forms, they follow from the real comparisons through the lemma below (derived). The actions and the forms are gauge invariant. For the minimisers and their lifts, which depend on the gauge, the lemma is applied to the representative given by the fixed point of VAR's contraction at the reference Ũ(z), which is analytic in z and at real z is the representative of the real bounds. The old terms of V(Y) are treated in "The room at complex points" above.
  - *Lemma (Two Constants).* Let 1 < 𝔯 < 𝔯₁, let 𝔣 be analytic on |z| < 𝔯₁ with |𝔣| ≤ 𝔐 there, and let |𝔣| ≤ 𝔪 on the real segment (−𝔯, 𝔯), with 0 < 𝔪 ≤ 𝔐. Then |𝔣(z)| ≤ 𝔪^{𝔢(z)}𝔐^{1−𝔢(z)} for |z| < 𝔯, with 𝔢(z) := 1 − (2/π)|arg((𝔯 + z)/(𝔯 − z))|. For 𝔯 := 24 and 𝔯₁ := 25, |𝔣(z)| ≤ 𝔪^{15/16}𝔐^{1/16} for |z| ≤ 1.
  - *Proof.* On the upper half-disc D₊ := {|z| < 𝔯, Im z > 0}, 𝔴 := (𝔯 + z)/(𝔯 − z) maps D₊ onto the open first quadrant, the segment onto the positive real axis, and the semicircle onto the positive imaginary axis. So 𝔥 := 1 + (2i/π)Log 𝔴 is analytic on D₊, and Re 𝔥 = 𝔢 is 1 on the segment and 0 on the semicircle. Let 𝔉 := 𝔣𝔪^{−𝔥}𝔐^{𝔥−1}. Then |𝔉| = |𝔣|𝔪^{−𝔢}𝔐^{𝔢−1} is bounded on D₊, continuous on its closure except at ±𝔯, and ≤ 1 on its boundary there. For η > 0 let 𝔉_η := 𝔉·((𝔯² − z²)/(4𝔯²))^η, with the principal power, since Re(𝔯² − z²) > 0 on D₊. Then |𝔉_η| ≤ 1 on the boundary and 𝔉_η → 0 at ±𝔯, so |𝔉_η| ≤ 1 on D₊ by the maximum modulus principle, and η → 0 gives |𝔣| ≤ 𝔪^𝔢𝔐^{1−𝔢} on D₊. On the lower half-disc the same follows for z ↦ conj(𝔣(z̄)), since 𝔢(z̄) = 𝔢(z), and on the segment it is the hypothesis. For |z| ≤ 1, |arg(𝔯 ± z)| ≤ arcsin(1/𝔯), so 𝔢(z) ≥ 1 − (4/π)arcsin(1/𝔯), which exceeds 15/16 at 𝔯 = 24, since (4/π)arcsin(1/24) < 0.054. Since 𝔪 ≤ 𝔐, 𝔪^𝔢𝔐^{1−𝔢} = 𝔐(𝔪/𝔐)^𝔢 ≤ 𝔪^{15/16}𝔐^{1/16}. For 𝔪 > 𝔐 the bound 𝔪^{15/16}𝔐^{1/16} ≥ 𝔐 holds trivially, so the Lemma applies with any real-segment bound 𝔪.
  - *The lines.* Let (𝐔, 𝐉) be in the class, with 𝐔 = e^{iξA′}U as in CRE (2.39), U G-valued, A′ = A′_R + iA′_I, and 𝐉 = 𝐉_R + i𝐉_I, the four fields real. Let 𝐔(z) := e^{iξ(A′_R + zA′_I)}U and 𝐉(z) := 𝐉_R + z𝐉_I. Then (𝐔(i), 𝐉(i)) = (𝐔, 𝐉), and the line is real for real z. Since ∇_U is a real operator, |A′_R| and |A′_I| are ≤ |A′|, and |∇_UA′_R| and |∇_UA′_I| are ≤ |∇_UA′|. Both runs are evaluated at the common data D(z) := M^·(𝐔(z)) at the shared scales, since Bałaban's extension replaces the variables V_k in U_0 by the averages M^k(𝐔) (LFR II p.358 l.27–29). 𝐔 lives on one run's lattice (CRE p.256 l.58), and only its averages at the shared scales enter, which are data of both runs. The integration variables (V′ on Λ, B, and V″) and the couplings are fixed and equal in both runs, with B real and |B| ≤ b_k, as in (b)–(d). The line 𝐉(z) plays no role in (b)–(d), whose minimisers carry their own currents, or J′ at the alignment, and whose forms sit at these minimisers; 𝐉(z) enters only the old terms of V(Y), in "The room at complex points". The class of the norms of (LF-2) is the multiscale class Ũ^c of CRE (2.39); Bałaban's extension uses the single-scale class U_k^c(Z, α_{0,k}, α_{1,k}).
  - *The real points.* For real z with |z| < 24, ξ|A′_R + zA′_I| ≤ 25ξ|A′| and ξ²|∇_U(A′_R + zA′_I)| ≤ 25ξ²|∇_UA′|, with the bounds of (2.39). With (2.34)–(2.37), the plaquettes, currents, and averages of the line at a point of the layer m are therefore ≤ C_c(α_{0,m} + α_{1,m}) times the scale factors of (2.34)–(2.37), with C_c := 60: the linear terms give at most 2·25, and the quadratic ones, which carry a further factor α_{1,m}, at most 10 for γ small. So the real points of the line are real configurations whose data are regular with ε₁′ := C(M)R_kε′_k, where ε′_k := ε_k + C_c(α̂₀ + α̂₁) + 25g_kb_k and α̂_i := max_{m≤k}α_{i,m}. Hence (b)–(d) hold there with ε₁′ in place of ε₁, under the following condition.
    - (T-c): the conditions of (T-Λ) hold with 26ε₁′ in place of ε₁, 26C_c(α̂₀ + α̂₁) ≤ min(α₁, α_J), with α₁ and α_J of the pair class, and the thresholds of VAR Sects. A–E hold for the pairs of VAR (172)–(175) (p.305) with ε₁′ for ε₁. There C₁ := C_H is a constant with |V′ − 1| < C_Hε₁′ for the data of the disc: the line moves the data by ≤ 26ε₁′ and the chain's arguments are ≤ 26C_Ψε̂′ ≤ 52C_Ψε₁′, so C_H := 2(26 + 52C_Ψ) serves, by |e^{iX} − 1| ≤ 2|X| for |X| ≤ 1. With B₅ := 6B₁B₃C_H (173), ε₂ := 8B₅ε₁′ (below (175), p.305), and ε₃ := 4ε₂ (113) (p.294 l.42–45), the thresholds are 32B₅ε₁′ ≤ min(a₃, ½c₄, (18C₂B₀dc₁(½))^{−1}) (Proposition 4, p.292 l.50, and p.289 l.31–32) and 3B₀C_HB₃ε₁′ ≤ a₄ (Proposition 6, p.295 l.45–49), with Bałaban's absolute constants a₃, a₄, c₄, B₀, B₁, B₃, and C₂. Under them Proposition 9 (p.309 l.8–14) gives 𝐇 with (19)–(21) at ε₂ = B₅ε₁′.

    This follows the pattern of Bałaban's extension of p.358 l.27–31, whose bounds "hold with ε_k replaced by O(α_{0,k} + α_{1,k})". The line's real points carry up to 25 times the class bounds and lie outside his class U_k^c(Z, α_{0,k}, α_{1,k}), so their regularity with ε₁′ is the derivation above, not his statement; (b)–(d) use only that regularity. (T-c) holds for γ small, uniformly in K. Indeed g(log g^{−2})^{q_i} increases for g small and g_m ≤ (1 + β₀)g_k for m ≤ k (CRE (2.6)), so α̂_i ≤ (1 + β₀)C_ig_k(log g_k^{−2})^{q_i}, and ε₁′ is g_k times a power of log g_k^{−2}, up to constants. Every threshold of (T-c) bounds ε₁′ or α̂₀ + α̂₁ by a constant independent of k and K, so all of them hold for γ small.
  - *The disc.* For |z| < 25 the line moves A′ and ∇_UA′ by at most 26 times their class bounds. Under (T-c) the line therefore lies in the analyticity domains of VAR Sect. G, as in (c), "The remainder", and of the pair class 𝒟̄. So the minimisers, their lifts, the actions, and the forms of (b)–(d) are analytic in z there. Each run's per-point action density is ≤ C_actg_{j(x)}^{−2}ε₁′² there, with C_act := d²(52C₀)², by |F| ≤ 52C₀ε₁′/a_j². Each run's forms are ≤ c_4N_k per point, as on the alignment's disc.
  - *The results.* At the point (𝐔, 𝐉), z = i, the Lemma gives the following bounds per point of the history.
    - The actions. At the real points, the per-point densities d_y = [a_y(U^A) − a_y(Ũ)] + b_y(U^B) of (c), "The comparison", at each configuration of (b)–(d) with its alignment field, are ≤ C_y(M)g_{j(x)}^{−2}(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁′². This follows from the first- and second-order bounds 4d²C₀ε₁′d̄′ and 4d²d̄′² of a block density under the alignment, d̄′ := C_U(M)(ε/a_n)^{(1−ε₀)/3}ε₁′, and from |b_y| ≤ C_b(ε/a_n)ε₁′², with C_y(M) := 4d²(C₀ + C_U(M))C_U(M) + C_b. On the disc they are ≤ 2C_actg_{j(x)}^{−2}ε₁′². So on the class |d_y| ≤ C_dc(M)g_{j(x)}^{−2}(ε/a_n)^{5(1−ε₀)/16}ε₁′², with C_dc(M) := max(C_y(M), 2C_act).
    - The forms. At the real points they differ by ≤ r̄_nN_k, by *The result for the forms.* with ε₁′ ≤ 1. On the disc they differ by ≤ 2c_4N_k. So on the class they differ by ≤ max(C_F(M), 2c_4)(ε/a_n)^{5(1−ε₀)/16}N_k per point.
    - The couplings. Through the weights they enter linearly, as A(w^B − w^A, ·), so on the class they are bounded directly by the weight difference times the disc bound of a density. Through the data exp(ig_kB) they enter through a data derivative times |g^A_k − g^B_k||B|, which Cauchy's estimate on the data disc of (c), "The remainder", bounds on the class with ε₁′ for ε₁. Both bounds keep the form of the real ones, linear in the coupling differences.
    - The absorption. g_{j(x)}^{−2}ε₁′² and N_k carry the same factors as in the result of (c): linear in k and powers of log g_k^{−2}. Since 5/16 exceeds 1/4, θ̃ ≥ L^{−(1−ε₀)/4} and k < 8n give L^{−5(1−ε₀)n/16} ≤ θ̃^nL^{−(1−ε₀)n/16}. These absorb the factors, so the changes on the class are ≤ C_hθ̃^n per point, with θ̃ unchanged.

    So the per-point bounds of (b) and (c) on the class, which the complex version of the Lemma of the Cauchy note's §8.6 needs for d, and the bounds of the four actions and of the form in 𝐂_k − W_ext, are derived on the class. The exception is the variation (ii) of V(Y) at the complex points.
- *The lift difference as a Taylor coefficient.* A bound of E without the Cauchy note's (10.6c), as the first Taylor coefficient of the alignment field along the data tg_kB, does not work, since the real bound of that field holds in the alignment's gauge, its first coefficient is computed in the Landau gauges of VAR Sect. G, and the first-order gauge mismatch between the two is what (10.6c) controls.

(e) *The Carried Boundary Terms (Derived at Real Backgrounds; Open at Complex Backgrounds, for the R-Operation's Own Terms, and for the Exceptional Deep V(Y)).* In (e), k is the fluctuation step, and k_s := K − j(C) is the stopping scale of the Cauchy note's §8.2, the k of §1, which sets the thresholds k_s/8 and k_s/4.
- *The objects.* A boundary term 𝐁^{(i)} of CRE (2.40)–(2.42) is localised in X ∈ 𝐃_i with X ∩ Ω_i ≠ ∅ and X ∩ Z_i^∼ ≠ ∅, and is bounded by B₀exp(−κd_i(X)) on its class (p.261 l.47–66). After the step that creates it, it is a fixed function, evaluated at the later backgrounds: the form 𝐁^{(i)}(U_k, …) of (2.40), with 𝐁_k(U_{k+1}) remaining in the action (3.25). The class norm of its two-run difference is b_i, the smallest number with |𝐁^{B,(i)}(X) − 𝐁^{A,(i)}(X)| ≤ b_iB₀exp(−κd_i(X)) on the class: the norm of the Cauchy note's §8.2 with B₀ in place of E₀. The class is the intersection of the two runs' classes Ũ^c_i(X, α̃₀, α̃₁), on which both runs' terms are analytic and bounded. The thresholds (2.28) carry each run's own g_i, and g^A_i and g^B_i differ relatively by ≤ Cg_i²|δ_i|, so the intersection contains either run's class with α̃₀ and α̃₁ scaled by 1 − C′g_i²|δ_i|, C′ a constant of q₀ and q₁. Here Z_i = Λ_i^c (p.255 l.25), and Γ_m = Ω_m^{(m)} ∖ Ω_{m+1}^{(m)} is the layer of the scale m (p.255 l.15–16). By (2.47) (p.263 l.50–p.264 l.8), the part 𝐁^{(i,m)} of the generation i on the layer Γ_m, m < i, carries the factor 2^{−(i−m)}, from exp(−d_i(X)). So the generations i ≥ m sum to ≤ 2B₁ per point of Γ_m, and their differences to ≤ 2B₁max_{i≥m}b_i. The generations i < m on Γ_m are not in (2.47); they carry the inward weight of "The weights of the ages" below.
- *Where they enter.* At a fluctuation step k, 𝐁_k enters CRE (3.26) only through its first expression: as the integrand 𝐁_k(U_k(…)) − 𝐁_k(U_{k+1}) of the t-integral, and, multiplied by t, in the measure (3.27) of that integral (p.271 l.7–8, l.22). The output of that expression goes into 𝐁^{(k+1)} only (p.271 l.29–31, p.279 l.16). The s-integral and the logarithm in (3.26) are taken at t = 0 (p.271 l.14–15), so the 𝐁-terms do not enter the step's 𝐄- and 𝐑-outputs; 𝐁_k(U_{k+1}) remains in the action as a frozen function. Two more expressions of the t-integrand go into the boundary terms only (p.277 l.10–17): the coupling term A(g_k^{−2}(·) − g_k^{−2}, ·), localised in Z_k, and V^{(k)}(S_{k+1}), the expressions above expanded in the perturbative terms of (1.22), each with a localisation in S_{k+1}. At an R-operation, the old terms whose domains meet a component Z belong to the first part of (1.49), which is integrated with Z (LFR II p.369 l.28–30). The terms disjoint from Z are expanded around the new background U_k. There 𝐓(X, U_k) stays as a frozen function (taken at U_k, p.373 l.5–6), and the brackets 𝐓(X, exp iη𝐇″_k(g_kB)U^0_k) − 𝐓(X, U^0_k) of (1.59)–(1.60), with their analogues along the chain of (d), give "a term which is small, and satisfies the exponential bound (2.42) [III] in d_k(Y₀)". Bałaban takes these "together with other terms of the first part of the effective action" (p.372 l.27–33), so they too are integrated with Z. So the R-operation carries the frozen boundary terms disjoint from the components with the factor 1. The boundary terms that it creates itself are those of the preliminary integrations of LFR I §1, localised in X ∈ 𝔻_m, for some m ≥ j + 1, with X ⊂ Ω_{m+1}^c, X ∩ Ω_m ≠ ∅, and X ∩ (Z_{j+1} ∖ Z″_{j+1}) ≠ ∅ (LFR I (1.63), p.189 l.40–47), and bounded by C₀exp(−(1 + 3β)κd_m(X)) on the spaces Ũ_k^{(n)c} (LFR I (1.70), pp.190–191). They are 𝔹_k^{(n)} = Σ_{i≤n}ℂ_k^{(i)}, with ℂ_k^{(n+1)} the logarithm of the integral (1.60) plus the coupling term A(1/(g_k^{(n+1)})² − 1/(g_k^{(n)})², U_k^{(n+1)}) (LFR I (1.61)–(1.62), pp.188–189). Z_{j+1} is identified with Z_{j+1} ∩ Z (LFR I p.177 l.33–35), so these terms meet the components renormalised at the step. Where the integrations of a component end at some n, Bałaban leaves them "as a part of all boundary terms" (LFR I p.191 l.44–47), and from then on they are carried like the others. So their two-run difference at creation is a source of the recursion below, and that source is open: it stays in (γ) of the Cauchy note's §8.6. What is missing: the two-run difference of ℂ_k^{(n+1)}. The integral (1.60) "is of the same type as the integral studied in Sect. 3 [III]" (p.189 l.36–38), so its difference is that of the marked expansion of the Cauchy note's (8.1) run for (1.60), with the frozen inputs as marked activities (among them the earlier 𝔹_k^{(i)}, i ≤ n), the soft shells of the characteristic functions of (1.60) as in §3(d), the depth of U_k^{(n)} by (b), the coupling term through the coupling differences as in "The step map" below, and the variation on the spaces Ũ_k^{(n)c}, whose room at complex points is the point (3) of (LF-A). A recursion over n ≤ N with Bałaban's cumulation of at most N₀ terms (p.191 l.49–p.192 l.6) then gives the source. What closes it: that marked expansion with these inputs, once §3(d) and the point (3) of (LF-A) are closed. The old 𝐁-terms enter the quotients of the Cauchy note's §8.6 through V(Y) of (d).
- *Deep terms.* A term is deep at a step i > k_s/8 if its domain contains a point of a layer ≤ k_s/8 at that step.
  - A boundary term of the generation i that is still carried at a later step contains a point of a layer ≤ i. Its domain X meets Z_i^∼, one layer of MR_i-cubes around Z_i (p.245 l.40–43), so X has a point y in an MR_i-cube touching Z_i. By (2.9), MR_ia_i ≤ (1 + L^{−1})MR_{i+1}a_{i+1} ≤ 2MR_{i+1}a_{i+1}, so y lies within three layers of LMR_{i+1}-cubes around Z′_i, the union of the LMR_{i+1}-cubes meeting Z_i (p.264 l.52–55). By (3.2)–(3.5), Ω_{i+1}^c ⊇ (B^{i+1}(P^1_{i+1})^c)^{∼3} ⊇ Z′_i^{∼8} (p.265 l.2, l.17–22, l.43), so y ∉ Ω_{i+1}. The layer of a point changes only when a component containing the point is renormalised, since an R-operation acts inside the renormalised components: its domain Λ = (Ω_{k₀+1}^{∼4})^c ∩ Z and the sets Z″_k ⊂ Λ lie in Z (LFR I p.192 l.43, p.195 l.42); then X meets that component, so the term belongs to the first part of (1.49) and is integrated with it (LFR II p.369 l.28–30). So y stays at a layer ≤ i while the term is carried.
  - The output domains of a step contain the domains of their inputs: at a fluctuation step the polymers of the cluster expansion are unions of the inputs' domains with further cubes (RG II p.12 l.9–11, l.23, (2.9), (2.13); CRE p.278 l.16–31), and at an R-operation the same holds for the expansions of LFR II p.387 l.40–42, p.388 l.2, l.13–14. So a term built from a deep input is deep, a non-deep term is built from non-deep inputs only, and by the first item a non-deep boundary term at a step > k_s/8 has a generation > k_s/8, as have its boundary inputs.
  - The norms D, S, and b of the Cauchy note's §8 at the steps > k_s/8 are taken over the non-deep terms. A deep term at a step j > k_s/8 has a point y at a layer ≤ k_s/8, and y ∈ Λ_j^c, since Λ_j ⊂ Ω_j ⊂ Ω_{k_s/8+1}. Let Z′ be the component of Λ_j^c containing y (LFR I p.177 l.16–17); its history reaches a layer ≤ k_s/8.
    - Let the term, of the generation i and with the domain X, meet a component Z ≠ Z′ renormalised at the step j > k_s/4. X is connected (RG I p.257 l.7–9), so it passes from y through Λ_j ⊂ Ω_j to Z, and crosses every layer Γ_l, k_s/8 < l < j. Bałaban's bound of exp(−d_i(X)) for a generation-i term with a point in Γ_n, ≤ 2^{−(i−1−n)} (p.264 l.1–3), gives exp(−d_i(X)) ≤ 2^{−(i−1−k_s/8)} for i > k_s/8 + 1. The layers i ≤ l < j cost ≥ R(j − i) π_i-cubes, and the layer j − 1 alone ≥ RL^{j−1−i}, by (a) of "The weights of the ages", so exp(−½δκd_i(X)) ≤ exp(−½δκRL^{j−1−i}), and with ½δκR ≥ σ ≥ (d + 1)log L + 1 this pays the L^{d(j−i)} points of the scale i in a point of the scale j up to a factor L^de^{−(j−1−i)}. Since j − k_s/8 > k_s/8, one of the two factors is ≤ L^de²2^{−k_s/16} at every generation. So the deep terms meeting Z are ≤ C_Dk̄Ē₀2^{−k_s/16} per point of Z at the scale j, with k̄ := max(k_s, j) ≥ j bounding the number of generations i ≤ j, Ē₀ := max(E₀, B₀, R₁) bounding the frozen 𝐄-, 𝐑-, and 𝐁-terms as in the Cauchy note's §8.6, and C_D a constant of C_X, L, and d, the rest of the exponent, exp(−(κ − 1 − δκ)d_i(X)), controlling the sum over X as exp(−(κ − 1)d_j(X)) does in Bałaban's (2.48) (p.263 l.50–52), with C_X the constant of that sum at the rate ½(κ − 1), which is ≤ κ − 1 − δκ for δ ≤ ¼ and κ ≥ 2; their two-run difference is ≤ twice this, by the triangle inequality. They enter ν_j of the Cauchy note's §8.6.
    - Otherwise the term meets no component renormalised at the step j other than Z′. If Z′ is renormalised, the term belongs to its first part and is bounded in the Cauchy note's §8.6, "Fine scales and old histories". If Z′ is of the second class, the term enters 𝐓′_k(Z′), an ingredient of Z′'s large-field operation, and stays deep with Z′ (LFR II p.378 l.8, l.20–22). If Z′ is not among the components renormalised at the step j (LFR I p.177 l.33), the term is carried with Z′ and stays deep. If Z′ is alive at the stopping scale, the pair is old and is bounded by the triangle inequality in the Cauchy note's §6.2. A deep term that meets a component renormalised at a step j with k_s/8 < j ≤ k_s/4 is bounded by the triangle inequality in the Cauchy note's §8.6, "Fine scales and old histories", which covers every component renormalised at a step ≤ k_s/4.
  - *Deep V(Y) in a Young Polymer (Derived, Except the Exceptional Constituents).* Let a component be renormalised at the step j > k_s/4, Bałaban's k in LFR II (1.61)–(1.68), and let V(Y) be a factor of LFR II (1.91) in a polymer X′ whose components of Bałaban's Z, those that X′ contains, are all young. By LFR II p.377 l.5–8, Y ∩ Z^∼ is a union of components of Z^∼, and these lie in X′, so every point of Y ∩ Z lies at a layer > k_s/8. Let Y be deep, with a point y at a layer ≤ k_s/8. Then y ∉ Z, and y ∈ Λ_j^c, since Λ_j ⊂ Ω_j ⊂ Ω_{k_s/8+1}. The component W of Λ_j^c containing y is not one of the components that make up Z (LFR I p.177 l.33), so W ∩ Z = ∅. In this item the old terms' generation is i, Bałaban's j in (1.66).
    - *The Constituents.* V(Y) re-sums, at fixed Y = Y₄, the terms of the expansions (1.61)–(1.64) of the old terms 𝐓(X) disjoint from Z, with Y₄ = Y₁ ∪ Y′₂ ∪ Y′₃ ∪ Y′₄ and Y₁ the smallest 𝐃_k-domain containing X and the components of Z that meet X (p.373 l.15–16), here the smallest 𝐃_k-domain Y₀ containing X. Each constituent has the factor (1.66) (p.376 l.19), and Y₄ ∖ Z = (Y₀ ∖ Z) ∪ ⋃_{i=2}^4(Y′_i ∖ Z) (p.376 l.23). So every constituent of a deep V(Y) has y in Y₀ ∖ Z or in some Y′_i ∖ Z.
    - *The Crossing.* Let a constituent have some Y′_i nonempty, i₀ the first such index. Each Y′_i connects Y′_i ∖ Z with Λ (p.376 l.24–25), and Λ = (Ω^{∼4}_{k₀+1})^c ∩ Z ⊂ Z (LFR I p.192 l.43). If y ∈ Y′_i, a path in Y′_i joins y to Λ. If y ∈ Y₀, X has a point y′ within one M-cube of the scale of y's layer, so at a layer ≤ k_s/8 + 1, since the layers are wider than RM in their own units (PRT2 (2.2), p.224 l.10–13). A path in X, which is connected (RG I p.257 l.7–9), joins y′ to a point of X, a contour realising the scaled distance d((Y₁⁰)^c ∩ Y₁ ∩ Y′_{i₀}, X) of the third term of (1.66) (PRT2 (2.46), p.231 l.9–13, cited at LFR II p.376 l.13–16) joins that point to Y′_{i₀}, and a path in Y′_{i₀} joins its end to Λ. The path leaves W only through Λ_j ⊂ Ω_j, before it reaches Λ ⊂ Z, so it crosses each layer Γ_l with k_s/8 + 1 < l < j inside W, outside Z: N := j − 2 − ⌊k_s/8⌋ layers at least. A layer is crossed by at most three consecutive pieces, in X, along the contour, and in Y′_{i₀}. The tree lengths and volumes are counted in the scale of each layer (CRE p.263 l.25–27; Bałaban's bound of exp(−d_i(X)) by 2^{−(i−1−n)}, p.264 l.1–3, rests on this). So pieces of extents t₁, t₂, and t₃ in the layer's units, with t₁ + t₂ + t₃ > RM, cost ≥ t₁/M − 1 of d_i(X), ≥ t₂ of the scaled distance, and ≥ t₃/M M-cubes of Y′_{i₀}. Half of the exponential that remains after Bałaban's extraction (p.376 l.37), exp(−½βκd_i(X) − ¼δd((Y₁⁰)^c ∩ Y₁ ∩ Y′_{i₀}, X) − Σ_i¼(κ₁ − 1)M^{−d}|Y′_i|), therefore gives e^{−σ_V} per crossed layer, with σ_V := c_VR − ½βκ and c_V := min(½βκ, ¼δM, ¼(κ₁ − 1)).
    - *The Bound.* The other half of that exponential controls Bałaban's resummations of p.376 l.39–42, over the Y′_i, the X, and the generations, as the whole does. Those sums need the rates only above the constants of the counting of trees, domains, and points (Sects. 6, 7 [I] and Sects. 2, 3 [III], p.376 l.42), which Bałaban's large κ and κ₁ also exceed at half the rates, and the δ-sum costs a factor of the type δ^{−d}, absorbed with the other constants in α, "arbitrarily small for γ small" (p.377 l.14). So the constituents of a deep V(Y) with a nonempty Y′_i sum to ≤ C_Vα e^{−σ_V max(N, 0)}exp(−(1 + 2β)κd_{k,Z}(Y)), with C_V a constant of the halved resummations. Under the condition σ_V ≥ 1 on Bałaban's R, of the kind of (⋆), and with j > k_s/4, N ≥ k_s/8 − 2, so e^{−σ_V max(N, 0)} ≤ e²e^{−k_s/8} ≤ e²2^{−k_s/16}. The two runs' sums of these constituents therefore differ by ≤ r_Dα exp(−(1 + 2β)κd_{k,Z}(Y)), r_D := 2e²C_V2^{−k_s/16}, by the triangle inequality.
    - *The Exceptional Constituents (Open).* The constituents with all Y′_i empty have only the first two terms of (1.66), with Y = Y₁ = Y₀ (p.376 l.43–46). Their Y need not reach Λ_j, so no crossing is forced, and at the deep points their variation is of the order of Bałaban's bound. What is missing: a bound of the two-run difference of the exceptional constituents of a deep Y. What closes it: their dependence on the R-operation's new data enters Y₁ only through the data of U^0_k on (Y₁⁰)^c ∩ Y₁, which carry exp iQ̃(η𝐇″_k), and on Λ ∩ Y₁ (p.373 l.20–21), so a bound of the decay of 𝐇″_k from Z to (Y₁⁰)^c ∩ Y₁ across the layers between them, at the rate of (b) of "The weights of the ages", gives the same crossing.
- *The step map.* At the fluctuation step k the boundary terms are among the activities of the expansion of the Cauchy note's (8.1), and its marking "R-mark" applies: the two-run difference of an output is a sum of terms, each linear in one marked activity, with the other activities at run B or at run A. So the two-run difference of 𝐁^{(k+1)} has three parts. (i) The terms with the frozen 𝐁-difference of one input marked. (ii) The terms with the step's own two-run difference marked, applied to run B's boundary terms: the measure difference of (8.1), which contains 𝐁_k through (3.27), and the variation of the old terms at the two runs' backgrounds. (iii) The 𝐁-parts of the output differences generated by the other marked activities. The t-integral is a product of the integrand 𝐁_k(U_k(…)) − 𝐁_k(U_{k+1}) and the measure (3.27), which contains t𝐁_k. Its two-run difference telescopes into the integrand's difference against run A's measure plus run B's integrand against the measures' difference, and the measures' difference is, by the interpolation of the Cauchy note's (8.1), a sum of terms each linear in one marked activity, t times a frozen 𝐁-difference among them. So R-mark applies with two insertions, one in the integrand and one in the measure, and every term is linear in one marked activity.
  - For (i): Bałaban bounds the expansion of the t-integrand 𝐁_k(U_k(…)) − 𝐁_k(U_{k+1}) as in (2.42) with the additional factor O(ε_k), "arising from the bound of the fluctuation field" (p.277 l.7–9), through the analyticity of the term in the fluctuation (RG I (3.10)–(3.14), p.272 l.6–44). The frozen difference of a term of the generation i is analytic on the same domain, being the difference of two such terms, with the bound b_iB₀exp(−κd_i(X)) there, so the same estimate gives C_εε_k times that bound, C_ε being Bałaban's constant in O(ε_k). Its place in the measure (3.27), multiplied by t, has the same bound. Bałaban's treatment of the 𝐁-terms by RG I (3.6)–(3.7) with the localisation over the Y₀ ∈ 𝐃_k meeting Λ_{k+1} (CRE p.276 l.42–46), the cluster expansion (3.44)–(3.47) (pp.277–278), and the three facts of the Cauchy note's §8.2 then give an output of the form (2.42), and by §8.5 Bałaban's derivation of (2.47) applies to it.
